### 08:57 to 09:35, the E4 tool in its first form, M2 prepared
- `npm run handover -- --stop ID [--kind dive|rise] [--tier draft|review] [--only sides,flow,frames,sheets,path] [--params FILE --dist DIR --name NAME]` (`tools/handover.ts`, image measures in `tools/img/handover.py`). Writes `out/work/handover/<name>.json`, `<name>.md`, `<name>-<kind>-sides.png` (close side, globe side, difference times `limits.differenceGain`), `<name>-<kind>-difference.png`, `<name>-<kind>-<tier>-before|across|after.png` (12 frames each, T minus 12 to T plus 12), frames cached in `out/work/handover/<name>/frames/` with `.stamp` files (engine and stop hashes). Exit 1 when a judged number is over its limit. `--only path` gives the per frame geometry without images; the default tier `review` adds review sheets, `--tier draft` leaves them out. A stop that has not opted in is judged with T at its cover centre and fails the sides part. One browser session, the views switched with `showcase.setDiagnostic`.
- Config names follow the spec: `handover.maxEnlarge` (3), `handover.maxRateOverMean` (2), `handover.limits` (all provisional until the frozen dive is measured): `sideMean`, `sideP99`, `flowStepFrames` (the flow is taken over this share of a frame on each side of T, so the sides' own acceleration does not count as a gap), `flowGapMeanPx`, `flowGapMaxPx`, `backRate` (the globe side may not gain height in a dive, or lose it in a rise, by more than this share of the mean rate), `earlyShare`, `earlyRateOverMean`, `placeMiddle`, `levelStep`, `colourStep`, `flatShare`, `flatFloor`, `blockPx`, `sheetFrames`, `differenceGain`. `src/config.ts` (not in my listed folders, but the config loader has to know the block) checks them.
- `src/director/moves.ts`: public `place(kind, time)`, the look target on the unit sphere. `src/core/motion.ts`: `rawMotion(open, mid, close, probe?)` with a probe per grid point for the tool. No change in behaviour.

### 10:10, plan for E3
- Two schedules in `glide()`: height (and field of view) keeps `descent.rise`, `descent.fall`; direction from the place and attitude get their own (`turnRise`, `turnFall`), leading the height in a dive so the camera comes down a line of sight to the place and the levelling is spread; mirrored in the rise. Per stop and kind override in `handover.dive.path` and `handover.rise.path`.
- Mars rise: earlier T (`handover.rise.at` in `src/stops/mars/plan.ts`) for a slower climb; a longer ease into the travel.
- Request from mars_place_builder (10:10): `MAX_PATCHES` 6 to 10 in `src/bodies/planet.ts` (8 to 9 levels of 2x steps hold G13 through both handovers).

Off by default. Do it right after A.

Where its weight k (placeWeight times the edge, honesty and enlargement feathers) is above 0.5 the pixel counts the picture (the close side's own density at T capped at 1, times picture texels per frame pixel on the longer screen axis); at 0.5 or below the pass discards and the globe's own levels are counted. So plates must hold G13 until placeWeight passes 0.5 and wherever the limits cut the picture.
- mars_place_builder's rule for E3: its pyramid holds G13 on the globe from height 0.0019 up at a pitch like T; below that the place picture must count the pixel, so placeWeight must be above 0.5 on every frame below height 0.0019, dive and rise. Its plates are laid back from one twin (the dive twin as on main); a rise twin that differs costs exactness on the rise. The terrain segment is frozen, so the rise twin is where the close camera is at the rise T.

### 11:30 to 12:00, the dive judged frame by frame, Phobos, the sidestep
- Rise draft (`out/work/e3/rise2.md`, T 2685): side difference at T mean 0.108, 99th 2; flow gap mean 0.075 px; mean ratio 1.15439, fastest 1.306 of the mean. Every frame looked at: the climb is steady, the horizon level until it leaves the top near 2707.
- Dive draft before the sidestep (`out/work/e3/dive2.md`, T 2454): side difference mean 0.091, 99th 1, 99.9th 3, max 130, 28 pixels over 16; flow 8.03 and 8.026 px a frame, gap mean 0.006, largest 0.01; mean ratio 0.87437, fastest 2438 at 1.311 of the mean, 2411 at 1.307; place at most 0.125 of the half frame from the middle (2428). Failed on the body: level step 20.56 into 2406, colour 29.86 into 2407, flat share 20.1 percent at 2406.
- Cause: Phobos. Centre `[-0.013945, 0.09475, 2.758338]` turned about `plan.axis` by `moonDegPerSecond` (-1.2) times (time minus holdMid), radius 0.061096. It hangs 0.665 radii in front of the hold camera and 0.0986 off its axis (at 1152, 773 px, radius 186 px in frame 2400). The steady descent passed 0.0727 radii from its centre and the moon filled a third of frame 2406 for that one frame. The M2 path on main has the same flash (level dip at 2406 in `out/work/handover/mars-e2.md`). The frozen build let the moon slide out by the bottom over 2400 to 2421.
- New option `sidestep` (off unless a stop asks): `handover: { dive: { path: { sidestep: { right, up, by } } } }`. `right`, `up` in radii in the frame of the far camera (the hold's last pose in a dive, the travel's first in a rise); the far pose is turned about the place centre by that amount over its own range, eased in by `smoother(x / by)` with x the share of the way to T, so the first frame and its speed are those of the hold. `Sidestep` and `PathOptions.sidestep` in `src/director/handover.ts`; `sidestep()` in `src/director/moves.ts`; tests "the sidestep of a path" in `tests/descent.test.ts`.
- Mars: tried right -0.15, by 0.11: the moon left by the right edge over 2400 to 2405 but showed its flank, where its lit picture is stretched (`out/work/e3/moon-step-crop-2404.png`). Dropped. Chosen: right -0.05, up 0.25, by 0.13 (`src/stops/mars/place.json` `dive.path`): the camera lifts over the moon as the frozen build did. Moon centre y 774, 795, 877, 1030, 1264 px over 2400 to 2404, out at 2405; least distance 0.1837 radii from its centre. Seen: `out/work/e3/moon-up-2400-2411.png`, `moon-up-crops.png`. Probe after: turn at most 6.6 degrees a frame, pitch rate at most 2.64, tilt rate at most 1.8.
- Place limits per stop and kind: `handover.dive.place` and `handover.rise.place` take `seen`, `maxEnlarge`, `enlargeSoft` beside `valid` (defaults `config.handover.place.seen`, `config.handover.maxEnlarge`, `config.handover.place.enlargeSoft`). `handoverOf` refuses values out of range. Test in `tests/place.test.ts`.
- E4 judges the body only (`tools/handover.ts`, numbers from `tools/img/handover.py`): per frame the share of the frame the body fills, its mean level and colour; the steps between frames are taken on the body where both frames show at least `limits.bodyShare` (0.05) of it. The whole frame steps are still reported, not judged (the globe swelling into the frame is not a cut).
- Second source of the strokes (rise, the new timing): frames 2697 to 2703, x 950 to 1150, y 280 to 300, the far canyon wall of the place picture. The picture lies on the sphere; as the camera climbs to h the far ground is stretched along the view by about h / hT (hT the twin height 7.5e-4), and the light gullies of the wall become strokes: 2.1 times at 2697, 3.4 at 2700, cut by `maxEnlarge` 3 (gone at 4.5) by 2703. Proof: `out/work/e3/f2701-three.png` (beauty, `noplace`, `mask`): the strokes are in the picture, not in the globe, and the mask is whole there.
- Cure for the second source, on Mars only (`src/stops/mars/place.json` `rise.maxEnlarge` 1.2, `rise.enlargeSoft` 0.4, with a `limitNote`): the far field of the rise picture is cut where it is enlarged over 1.2 times, gone at 1.68. Tried 1.5 and 0.6 first: the wall strokes stayed through 2698 to 2703 (`out/work/e3/rise3-2690-2707.png`). With 1.2 and 0.4 the far field leaves over 2692 to 2697 and only short marks stay at the far rim of the patch (`out/work/e3/rise4-2692-2706.png`). Why the texel measure alone is not enough: a wall laid back on the ground is a long smear whatever its texel size; it reads as a wall only from the twin. The real cure is a short place window (12 frames as in the dive), which needs plates that hold one texel a pixel from the rise twin's height (7.5e-4) up: two more levels than the eight planned. Told mars_place_builder.
- What the frames show until the plates land (dive 2443 to 2451, rise 2692 to 2708): the picture as a feathered patch on a flat red globe, and a grade shift from the globe's red to the picture's brown (dive body colour step 9.8 into 2449). With `noplace` the globe alone is flat red there: nothing in the engine draws it. MAIL 256 calls it the red smear of 2708 to 2712 (M2 timing).

### 13:10 to 13:50, a fault of E2's pass at the horizon, and its cure (the limb hold)
- Seen on dive frames 2445 to 2453 and growing through the rise from 2690: a dark line along the horizon, between the ground and the haze of the picture (8 pixels high at 2447).
- Why. The camera of the place window is above the twin, so its limb lies beyond the horizon of the picture. A sky ray between the two was below the twin's horizon, so the pass called it unseen (weight 0) and the globe's own sky showed: dark, with stars, where the close view has its bright dust haze. The ground between the twin's horizon and the limb (one to a few pixels, foreshortened) had no weight either.
- The picture's horizon itself (dumps `out/work/e4/matte/f2454-dump-image.png`, `-alpha.png`): haze meets haze near window row 540, no relief stands above the horizon row, the far mesas start 20 to 55 texels below it. So the row of the horizon may be reused freely.
- The cure, in `src/core/place.ts` (LAY and its mirror `placeUv`), three parts, all off when the reach is 0:
  1. Sky lift. `gap` = the camera's dip minus the twin's dip in the direction of the pixel (`uLimb`: camera up minus twin up, and the difference of the two sines). A sky pixel `own` above the limb looks the picture up `gap * (1 - smoothstep(0, 1, own / max(reach, 2 |gap|)))` higher along the twin's up: at the limb it reads the picture's horizon, at the reach it reads where the twin saw it. Symmetric: a camera below the twin squeezes. The weight of a sky pixel is then whole (`honest` 1).
  2. Ground clamp and hold band. A hit point beyond the twin's horizon looks up the horizon row of the picture at its own azimuth. `hold = 1 - smoothstep(limbHoldDeg[0], limbHoldDeg[1], angle under the limb)` and the weight is at least place weight times the edges times `hold`, so the band under the limb keeps the picture where `seen` and `maxEnlarge` would drop it.
  3. Derivatives. A lifted sky pixel and a clamped ground pixel take the size of a pixel in the picture from the algebra of a direction (`placeSkyGrad` is the mirror), not from `dFdx` and `dFdy`. Why: on the limb a 2 by 2 block holds sky and ground, the picture coordinate jumps inside it (16 texels at 2447), the smaller singular value of that false Jacobian falls under the `maxEnlarge` limit and the pixel loses its weight. Seen as single dark pixels at the stair corners of the limb in the dive (2447: 1421,310 and 1787,314) and as a dashed dark line in the rise (2696, x 1485 to 1900).
- Numbers: `config.handover.place.limbReachDeg` 0 (off film wide) and `limbHoldDeg` [0.25, 0.75]; a stop turns it on per kind with `handover: { dive: { place: { limbReachDeg: 8 } } }` and may give its own `limbHoldDeg`. Mars has 8 on both kinds, from `src/stops/mars/place.json` with a `limbNote`. Harness option `limb=DEG` on the diag string overrides the stop.
- Why off by default: a body without air has stars down to its horizon in both views, so there is nothing dark in the gap, and the lift would move the picture's stars against the globe's own (double stars near the limb). It is for a close view whose horizon carries a haze the globe side does not draw.
- Measured (draft, uncommitted tree then the commit): frame 2447 with `beauty/limb=0` has the bytes of the frame cached before the change (sha256 ba23180d3b60); with the hold no pixel within the limb rows is more than 25 levels darker than both its neighbours on 2447, 2696, 2700 (the test found 60 before on 2447, 198 on 2696). Pictures: `out/work/e4/limb/f2447-three-crop.png` (before, lift only, lift and hold), `f2696-hold-crop.png` (dashes) and `f2696-hold2-crop.png` (cured), `f2700-hold2-strip.png`.
- What it does not cure: in the rise the far field of the picture is cut by `maxEnlarge` 1.2 from 2692 on, so below the held band the globe's own ground shows, plain red brown until the plates agree with the picture. At 2700 the haze stands as a bright band on the right part of the limb (the Sun's side of the close view's sky), at weight 0.43.
- Also seen, not mine: frame 2444, a dark region with a straight edge from the right end of the limb down to the bottom edge. It is in the frame cached before the change too and the place weight there is 0.09: it is a level edge of the old Mars globe, replaced by the ladder of mars_place_builder.
- earth_builder (13:2x, no hurry): it wrote the share of the sky through its handover as its own function (`src/stops/earth/set.ts` `skyWeights(time)`, numbers in its `place.json` block `sky`) and asks for a hook `skyShare(segment, time)` on the stop plan that returns the weight of each sky layer, default as today. To do after M3, off by default.

Samples asked at review, dive 2442 to 2454: 128, 128, 128, 128, 128, 128, 107, 82, 59, 39, 23, 16, 16; after T 16 each. Rise 2685 to 2697: 36, 33, 24, 16, 24, 42, 64, 87, 110, 128, 128, 128, 128; before T 16 or 17 each. All 50 frames: 2443 samples, about 2 hours 45 minutes on this machine. The 24 frames across the two T: 694 samples, about 45 minutes. (Probe: `planOf(frame, 'review')` of `tools/lib/samples.ts`.)

Tools only, no hash change.
- For the stop builders: the review sheets of one stop cost between 45 minutes (across only) and close to 3 hours (all three) when the dive is as fast as Mars. Say so to /root: at the final tier the same frames ask up to 256 samples.

### 15:00 to 15:20, edgeIn: the picture comes in from the place outward
- The fault: the pass weights the whole picture by one number in time, so wherever the ground under it is not quite the same ground the picture's sides show as straight lines (its field ends 0.06 of the width right of the frame on Mars).

Weight 1 gives reach 0, so T is untouched. All shares 0 adds 0.0 to a float: the same bytes.
- Plumbing: `PlaceOptions.edgeIn` and its check in `src/director/handover.ts`, `PlaceState.edgeIn` in `src/core/types.ts`, the place state in `src/director/film.ts`, the required key and check in `src/config.ts`, harness option `in=L,R,B,T` in `src/diagnostics/probe.ts` (applied in `src/core/engine.ts`), tests "the picture coming in from the place outward" and the option string in `tests/place.test.ts`.

### 15:20 to 16:00, the rise leaves from the eye of the dive (`fromDive`)
- 60c083e (merge of main 5eba228, edgeIn, `--gate-under`, the Sun's test fix) is on main as 0eae595 (MAIL 294): all 115 reference frames the same bytes on twelve machines, final 2325 and 2580 the kept stills, 170 of 170 tests there. Mercury is on main (d410b74).
- A mistake of mine, told to /root at 15:50: the draft run `mars-in1` (15:14) did not have edgeIn on. `src/stops/mars/plan.ts` builds the place options field by field and did not pass `edgeIn` from `place.json`; its frames were the bytes of `mars-ladder4`. Rule: after setting an option, check one frame's bytes changed before a long run.
- Why the rise failed by eye, measured with `out/work/tmp/risepath.ts` in the plates tree (prints the rise path per frame against the dive twin's eye): the plates are laid back from the eye of the dive twin (height 1.7193e-3 radii, pitch -17.75, roll -1.12). The old rise twin (T 2685) stood 2.31e-3 radii ahead of it, 0.38e-3 right, 0.97e-3 below: 1.47 twin heights away. The terrain path of the rise never climbed above 1.2e-3. So the picture of the rise was drawn from one eye over plates true from another: stripes through the cliff, a patch with straight sides. No plate can cure that.
- The cure: `handover: { rise: { path: { fromDive: POWER } } }`. `PathOptions.fromDive` in `src/director/handover.ts` (checked in `handoverOf`: throws on a dive, under 1, not finite, or when the stop has no dive segment); `private fromDive(own, time)` in `src/director/moves.ts` wraps the terrain branch of `risePose`. From the start of the rise the stop's own close path is led to `divePose(diveT, 'terrain')` by weight `s^POWER`, `s = (time - start) / (T - start)`, not clamped beyond T (so the coast and the glide go on at the rate the camera had). `twinFrame` is a rigid map, so an equal close pose is the same eye on the body. Without the option the pose comes back untouched: the same bytes for every other stop. Tests: `tests/descent.test.ts`, "a rise that leaves from the eye of the dive" (4).
- Mars values (in `src/stops/mars/place.json`, passed on by `src/stops/mars/plan.ts`): rise `at` 0.2973 (T frame 2692), `placeFrames` 12 (window 2692 to 2704), `fromDive` 2.2, `edgeIn` [0.4, 0.4, 0.4, 0.25] on both kinds; the rise's `maxEnlarge` and `enlargeSoft` are gone (the camera only recedes from the twin).

Under the picture 21 (tone 15), a warning. The rise now mirrors the dive in height: rise frame f is at the height of dive frame 5144 minus f.

### 16:15, the cure for the strokes: `follow` (in the scratch tree, not pushed yet)
- Idea: in the place window draw the place picture again for each sample from the eye of the globe camera itself, carried into the close set by the inverse of the twin (`StopPlan.untwin`), in place of the one eye of T. The pass then lays the picture from the camera onto the camera: the identity (shift 0, heights 1, limb gap 0), so nothing is laid flat and nothing smears. At T the two eyes are one eye: the sides stay equal.
- As written in `repo-plates` (ef93f79 plus this patch, 176 of 176 tests, tsc clean): `HandoverSide.follow` and `Handover.follow` (`src/director/handover.ts`, also `forceFollow` and `followForced` for the harness), `PlaceState.eye` (`src/core/types.ts`, the eye in the close set's own frame), `untwin` (`src/director/moves.ts`), the place state in `src/director/film.ts` (twin is the camera, eye is `untwin(local, time)` with the drift of the sample), `Engine.picture()` in `src/core/engine.ts` (the eye placed by the terrain set's state at T, the picture rebuilt for each sample like a live one), harness option `follow=1` or `follow=0` on the diag string (`src/diagnostics/probe.ts`), two tests in `tests/place.test.ts`.
- A stop asks with `handover: { dive: { follow: true } }`. Without it no `eye` is set and the code path is the old one.
- The eye's range on Mars (`out/work/tmp/followeye.ts` in the plates tree; close set units, the home eye of T is -0.183 0.218 1.294): dive 2444 at weight 0.09 stands 2.78 behind and 0.24 above, 2446 (0.29) 1.50 behind, 2448 (0.53) 0.71; rise 2696 (0.82) 1.15, 2698 (0.59) 1.97, 2700 (0.35) 3.14, 2702 (0.13) 4.85. The close set is a painted relief (`src/terrain/relief.ts`), true from its home eye: the question is what it shows from that far back (its near edge, stretched faces).
- Next: render 2444, 2446, 2448 and 2696, 2698, 2700 with `beauty/follow=1` in the plates tree and look beside the `mars-fd2d` and `mars-fd2` frames.

### 16:50 to 17:10, the tone split (`toneIn`)
- Idea: the picture is split in two. Its detail comes in as before (weight by time, edgeIn). Its broad tone (a wide blur of the frame) is the globe's own near the sides of the picture and the picture's only toward its middle, over a feather far wider than the detail's. The island of fault (a) is the picture's broad tone ending at the feather of the detail; with the tone on its own wide feather there is no rim to see.
- Simulation first, in numpy on rendered draft frames of 4f7b2dd2 (`/tmp/tone/sim.py`, `sim2.py`, `sim3.py`; F from the E4 frames, plates alone with `beauty/noplace`, the weight with `beauty/mask`): with the whole tone taken from the plates the island is gone at a blur of 24, 48 and 96 px; a weight by time alone (tone following the weight) does not help the rise, because at 2694 to 2696 the weight is still 0.97 to 0.82 while the side of the picture is already inside the frame. So the feather is in space: reach = share times (1 minus weight) to a power times the frame. With the power 0.5 the reach opens at a steady speed from T (the weight leaves 1 with a square).
- Engine (`src/core/place.ts`): `toneReach(place, layout)`, `toneGrid(width, height)`, a second shader `lay(true)` (the first, `lay(false)`, is the shader as it was, character for character; a test takes the additions out again and compares), a blur shader. Per sample, only when a reach is above 0: mode 4 writes per cell of 8 frame pixels the frame under the picture (16 taps) times how far the picture counts there (its weight over the weight by time) and that share; mode 5 the same of the picture; each is blurred along both axes (sigma `toneReach` of the frame's height); then the picture is laid with `lent = 1 minus the wide feather`: where the globe's broad tone is the lighter the picture is lifted by `lent` times the difference, where it is the darker the picture is scaled by the ratio (which can not go under zero and keeps the hue).
- A first form that only added the difference turned the limb glow teal at the right edge of 2696 (red pushed under zero where the picture's haze is far brighter than the globe). The lift and scale form has none of it.
- Numbers: `handover.place.toneIn` (four shares, default all 0: off), `tonePower` 0.5, `toneReach` 0.045, `toneCell` 8, checked in `src/config.ts`. Per stop and kind: `place: { toneIn: [left, right, bottom, top] }`. Harness: `tone=L,R,B,T` on the diag string.
- At T the weight is 1, every reach is 0 and the pass is the first shader: the two sides stay equal. While no stop asks, the second shader is never compiled.

### 17:10 to 17:25, the tone split measured in the engine (shares 0.5), and the shares opened to 4
- Mars rise with `tone=0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5` against today, draft stills on 4f7b2dd2 plus the patch (`/tmp/tone/eNNNN-t05b.png`): the rim of the island is softer at 2695, 2696 and 2698, but the slab still stands at 2694 and 2695 (straight lower rim). Broad difference to the globe alone (blur 24 px, mean of the absolute value, levels): 2694 11.92 to 10.96, 2695 10.47 to 8.93, 2696 8.80 to 6.99, 2698 5.26 to 3.74; dive 2446 2.15 to 1.37, 2448 4.98 to 3.41, 2450 8.78 to 6.83. The slope of that difference (99th percentile, levels per 100 px) hardly moves: 2695 79.3 to 77.4.
- Why so little: the reach is a share of the picture's valid size times (1 minus weight) to the power 0.5, and in the rise the weight is still 0.97 at T plus 2 and 0.82 at T plus 4 (the weight follows s = (n minus 0.8) / 12 through a smoothstep), so with a share of 0.5 the reach is 0.09 and 0.21 of the picture there. The simulation that removed the island lent the whole tone.
- The power stays 0.5: near T the weight leaves 1 with a square, so the reach opens at a steady speed (share times 0.144 of the picture per frame) and nothing jumps at T. A smaller power would open with an endless speed at T. What a stop sets is the speed, so the shares may now go up to 4 (config, per stop and the harness option): above 0.5 the two feathers meet in the middle and the whole tone is lent once the weight is low.
- Mercury dive 738 to 741 with `tone=0.5,0.5,0.5,0.5` (`/tmp/tone/eNNN-mt05.png` against `eNNN-mtoday.png`): the pale slab is clearly weaker, its tone near the sides is the globe's; what is left of the outline is the change of detail at the side (Mercury has no edgeIn) and a rest of tone at 741.

### 17:58 to 18:40, E6: the capped shutter (MAIL 310 and 313, decision d129)

- Asked: in the dives and rises of a stop that opted in the shutter of a frame is scaled by `k = min(1, allowed / raw)` around the middle of the full shutter (`raw` as `framePlan` measures it, the larger of both sides in the dissolve frame); keys `handover.maxBlurPx` (6) and `handover.blurEaseFrames` (12); nothing may step at a seam; everything else keeps its bytes; the report prints and judges; unit tests. The rounds of the critics wait for it.
- The ease is my form, agreed in MAIL 313: the blur a frame may keep rises from `maxBlurPx` to the raw motion of the segment's own first or last frame over `blurEaseFrames` (smooth ramp), only where that frame moves more than `maxBlurPx`. That frame then keeps its full shutter (scale exactly 1) beside a neighbour that always ran the full shutter. Where the seam moves less the cap lets go by itself: no ramp. Today one seam needs it: Uranus dive 4199 (10.04 px, terrain 4200 has 9.64), ease frames 4188 to 4199.
- One step beyond the letter of `allowed / raw` (told /root): where a move starts or ends inside the shutter the motion is not even across it, and `allowed / raw` alone left up to 3.6 percent more than allowed (Mercury 990: 6.217 px, Mars 2692: 6.024). The share is therefore corrected twice by the motion measured across the shortened shutter itself (`REFINE` in `src/core/motion.ts`); the motion left then equals the allowed one to 2.4e-6 relative at every capped frame of the film.
- Measured with the cap at 6 (`out/work/handover/e6-STOP-path.md`, written by `npm run handover -- --stop STOP --only path --name e6-STOP-path`; capped frames, least share of the shutter and its frame, most motion across the full shutter, review samples of the segment before and now):

  - earth.dive 72 of 90, 0.1455 at 1882, 41.24 px, 3862 to 1440. earth.rise 58 of 75, 0.1092 at 2133, 54.94 px, 3830 to 1200.
  - mars.dive 53 of 90, 0.041 at 2440, 146.69 px, 6241 to 1440. mars.rise 68 of 75, 0.0434 at 2705, 138.75 px, 6973 to 1200.

  - neptune.dive 77 of 90, 0.1508 at 4712, 39.8 px, 4245 to 1440. neptune.rise 67 of 75, 0.0855 at 4974, 70.17 px, 6156 to 1200.
- Seams (own frame with the full shutter and now, the frame beside it; px): sun.rise 180 3.65 and 3.65 beside terrain 179 3.67, 269 0.38 beside hold 270 0.2. mercury.dive 690 0.14 beside hold 0.24, 779 3.92 beside terrain 3.89; rise 960 2.66 beside terrain 2.65, 1034 0.21 beside travel 0.14. earth.dive 1830 0.22 beside hold 0.1, 1919 1.29 beside terrain 0.74; rise 2100 3.03 beside terrain 1.37, 2174 0.94 beside travel 0.96. mars.dive 2400 0.58 beside hold 0.35, 2489 3.06 beside terrain 3.07; rise 2670 3.78 beside terrain 4.06, 2744 0.76 beside travel 0.91. uranus.dive 4110 0.32 beside hold 0.2, 4199 10.04 beside terrain 9.64 (eased); rise 4380 4.67 beside terrain 4.73, 4454 0.44 beside travel 0.17. neptune.dive 4680 0.5 beside hold 0.41, 4769 1.06 beside terrain 1.01; rise 4950 0.69 beside terrain 0.74, 5024 0.54 beside the retreat 0.35. No seam frame is changed by the cap: every one keeps its full shutter.

It is the E3 rate limit applied to a stop that has not opted in. Not a change of E6, the tool stays as it is.
- An addition to the answer on the rim (sent 19:00, low priority, nothing built): what the eye bridges is blur against step, and that ratio is the same everywhere in the frame: blur / step = 0.5 times the share of the shutter. A film camera runs a share of 1 (180 degrees) down to about 0.25 (45 degrees, the staccato look, still read as motion). With `maxBlurPx` 6 the fastest frames run 0.041 (Mars 2440), 0.067 (Mercury 1003), 0.075 (Uranus 4186), 0.146 (Earth 1882); with 14 Mars 2440 runs 0.095. The knob that answers a strobe directly is a floor on the share: one key (for instance `handover.minShutter`, 0 for off) and `scale = max(allowed / raw, floor)` beside the cap. Because the motion is radial that gives a sharp middle and a streaked rim: with a floor of 0.25 Mars 2440 keeps 4.6 px inside the middle quarter, 12 px inside the middle half, 37 px at the corner; Earth 1882 keeps 2, 4.5 and 10 px. The review tier then needs up to 62 samples in the fastest Mars frames (37 / 0.6), 16 now, 128 before E6. The cap measured over a middle region is the other form (about 30 lines, the probe of `rawMotion` reports every grid point); both can stand side by side: `scale = max(min(1, allowed / rawOfTheRegion), floor)`. /root decides on its clips; do not build before.
- Final tier, the judged frames (`npm run samples -- --tier final --frames ...`): 247, 717, 1016, 1857, 2156, 2427, 2726, 4137, 4436, 4707, 5006 take 64 samples each with the cap at 6 (shares of the shutter 0.30, 0.10, 0.08, 0.21, 0.24, 0.06, 0.06, 0.14, 0.10, 0.15, 0.12).

The limb of the camera: row 299 at 2693, 218 at 2704, 11 at 2711. - The design (`limbSky`): the pass draws `under * (1 - k) + k * (sky * (1 - A) + RGB)`. Today k for the picture's sky is the weight of the picture times its side feathers, so the horizon of the picture leaves with the picture (and first, with edgeIn at the top). With `limbSky` the rays that miss the ball take `h = max(weight of the picture, share of the close view's sky)` and nothing else: no sides, no limits of enlargement, no land matte. (The first version, 9f850d9, gave the band just under the limb, `limbHoldDeg`, the same weight and clamped the uv of a ray that leaves the picture; both were dropped at 21:24, see the next section.) The share of the close view's sky is the clock on which the two star layers already cross, so the limb of the globe, the haze over it and the stars behind it change as one thing over `skyFrames` (or the stop's own `skyShare`) and reach the picture's values at T.
- It needs the picture's horizon on the camera's limb: `limbReachDeg` above 0 (checked in `handoverOf` and in `src/config.ts`).
- Code: `PlaceState.horizon` (types.ts), `PlaceOptions.limbSky`, `Handover.limbSky`, `horizonWeight`, `forceLimbSky` (handover.ts), the place state made also when the horizon has weight (film.ts, engine.ts), a second shader built only when asked (`VEIL_UNIFORMS`, `VEIL_SIDE`, `VEIL_WEIGHT`, `veilPass` in place.ts; the plain shader is the one it was, character for character), `horizonK` as the mirror for tests, diag `limbsky` and `limbsky=off` (probe.ts), `handover.place.limbSky: false` in the config with its check. Tests: `tests/limbsky.test.ts` (8) and two lines in `tests/place.test.ts`; 223 of 223 in 30 files.
- The byte proof, before: 45 draft frames of every dive and rise that hands over on a9f7d7a (`/tmp/hz/before.sh`, sha lines in `/tmp/hz/before-run.log`).

### 21:15 to 21:30, `limbSky` first try judged, the second version (c148dd1): the sky alone, the side turn

- First try (9f850d9) on Mars, ninth bake, draft, rise 2690 to 2716 (`/tmp/hz/m9/rise-off/`, `/tmp/hz/m9/rise-on/`; sheets `c-rise-off-L1.png`, `c-rise-on-L1.png`, `c-rise-on-L2.png`, `s-rise-on-a.png` in looks at 21:23). Off: the globe's hard bright limb line comes in under the haze from 2695 at the left and is a clear line by 2697. On: the haze stays on the clock of the sky and the line is far fainter, but a dark stripe lies under the limb at 2698 to 2704. It is the picture's own horizon row, held in the band under the limb (`limbHoldDeg`) at the weight of the horizon, on the globe's lit ground. Decision: only the rays that miss the ball take the weight of the horizon. The ground, the band under the limb too, keeps the weight the picture gives it.
- The side turn. The limb is a curve in the picture (on Mars about 2 px per degree of azimuth beyond a side), so a ray of the sky outside a side cannot read the nearest texel: a plain clamp of uv would read ground above the limb. In the second version such a ray (and one that looks away from the picture) is turned about the line to the centre of the body, in the frame of the twin, until it meets the nearer side plane of the picture at its own height over the twin's level; of the two crossings the one in front is taken. Then uv is clamped by half a texel, so over the top it reads the top row. `horizonUv(g, proj, ray, size)` in place.ts is the JS mirror of the shader's `VEIL_SIDE`; `placeUv` now also returns `dir`.
- How often a ray of the sky leaves the picture (`/tmp/hz/uvspan.ts STOP [REACH]`, rays 0.5 and 2 degrees over the limb across the frame, over the whole clock of the sky): Mars never (u 0.08 to 0.89, v 0.64 to 0.70, dive and rise); Mercury dive 722 and 724 a few (worst 1.2 degrees at h 0.08); Earth (skyFrames 120, horizon weight from 1830, limb in frame from about 1882) and Uranus none; Jupiter dive: the limb is above the top of the frame near T (rows minus 298 to minus 389), so no sky in frame there.
- Tests: `tests/limbsky.test.ts` now 9 (the ray test: sky takes h, ground keeps its own, in the band too; the side test: inside equals `placeUv`, outside the side half a texel in at the same height over the twin's level to 1e-9, a ray behind gets the front side, high over the picture the top row; the shader test: three additions taken out give the plain and the tone shader character for character, and nothing of the hold is in the addition).
- Disk: at 21:26 the renders refused (2.7 GB free). Deleted `/tmp/tone/fab`, `/tmp/tone/merc`, `/tmp/sb`, `/tmp/edgein`, `/tmp/hz/b`, `out/work/{seq,e2,e3,e4,e4-4f7b,edgein}` in `repo` and `out/work/{handover,edgein,e4}` in `repo-plates`: 4.3 GB free. `.git` is 4.6 GB (the plates of nine bakes); not packed again, a repack needs room it does not have.
- Scratch for the sheets: 1923c94452173083cab3b4f1e60c180763844309 (c148dd1 with db0c017, not pushed), checked out and built in `repo-plates`. Chain `/tmp/hz/mars2.sh` (21:27): rise on 2690 to 2716 into `/tmp/hz/m9/rise-on2`, dive on and off 2428 to 2456 (`dive-on2`, `dive-off`), eight masks (`mask-on2`), then the byte proof after in `repo` (45 frames, plain beauty, into `/tmp/hz/after`, log `/tmp/hz/after-run.log`, compare with `/tmp/hz/before-run.log`).

### 21:30 to 22:25, `limbSky` third step: the lap of the ball, told by the depth of the frame (bd87601), MAIL 349

- MAIL 349 (21:43): the shutter test of tests/shutter.test.ts fixed and pushed alone (84544e5, head cbb31e6), reported 21:42.
- The second version (c148dd1) left a bright line of 1 to 2 px on the limb in the rise 2696 to 2704: the mesh of the globe (`SphereGeometry(1, seg, seg / 2)`, flat cells) lies inside its sphere by 0 to (pi / seg) squared radii (9.64e-5 for seg 320: 1.2 px at 2699 in mid frame, up to 2 px at the sides), so a ray that meets the sphere there finds no mesh, the pass takes it for ground and the globe's own sky, brightest on the limb, shows. Other meshes: `globe.ts` seg 192, `moonbody.ts` seg 448, Venus circumscribed.
- A plain lap (`handover.place.limbLap`, 0.0001, in radii; a ray whose b * b - c is not above r * r * lap * (2 - lap) is sky) takes the bright line away but leaves darker dashes: where the mesh IS there inside the lap, the picture's dark horizon row is laid at the weight of the sky on the globe's lit ground. No single number cures both: lap 0.00005 left 759 bright columns, 0.0002 gave 233 dark ones (2699).
- So the pass asks the frame who drew the pixel. The veil pass is drawn twice with complementary depth tests, each pixel once: `EqualDepth` (nobody wrote depth: the lap under the sphere is sky, `uVeil.y = max(lap, 0)`), then `NotEqualDepth` (something wrote depth: ground, `uVeil.y = min(lap, 0)`). The veil shader writes `gl_FragDepth = 1.0` (in VEIL_WEIGHT, before the first return and the discard). With lap 0 one draw as before. JS mirror `lapOf(g, drawn)`, `placeUv(g, proj, ray, drawn)`, `horizonUv(..., drawn)`. A globe that writes no depth (Jupiter `globe.ts`, Mercury, the Uranus deck) is sky throughout its lap: it sets `limbLap: 0` or lives with it. In the tone split path only the last draw is tested.
- Measured, Mars rise at draft (`/tmp/hz/m10`, `/tmp/hz/m11`; `/tmp/hz/limbprof.py`): line on the limb, median over columns, 2699: off 8.3, no lap 26.7, plain lap 5.3, depth aware 5.7; 2702: 5.0, 15.0, 5.3, 5.7; 2696: 5.0, 10.3, 1.7, 2.0. Lap 0.0001 and 0.0002 give the same bytes with the depth test (the test decides, not the number). Crop at five times size, 2699 left of frame (`/tmp/hz/c-depth-zoom.png`, in looks 22:23): off a hard stepped limb, plain lap dark dashes, depth aware clean.
- Tests: `tests/limbsky.test.ts` 9 (lapOf, drawn rays, the two draws with their depth functions and laps, the tone path, the shader text with the depth line). vitest 224 of 224, build green.
- Pushed 22:23: bd87601 (the lap), d71c835 (merge of main 1ce3137). The merge conflicted in nine files because main took E6 as one commit of its own (25bbe77): main's versions of all nine equal 79de0c3f, which is an ancestor of mine, so mine were taken whole.
- NOT done for limbSky: sheets of every stop, the byte proof of 51 draft frames (before is in `/tmp/hz/before2-run.log`, base B0 1fb0aa0b). Told /root at 22:30 that plan F replaces limbSky by one function of height and asked whether it still wants them.

### 22:25 to 22:40, the second review note (d147, MAIL 351): plans E and F are mine

- Brandon's second note (about 21:50, on main 291a78b played live): lag at the change of view on Mercury, Venus, Earth, Jupiter; Mars "zooms in on a flat part of the planet, and then the actual landscape comes in"; Neptune "appears all of a sudden". He will capture the film himself at 3840 by 2160 and 60 frames a second, frame by frame, so the cost of a frame is no limit. Section "Second review note of 2026-11-23" in kit/PROGRESS.md on main 1ce3137 (plans E to I).
- What the code does today (read 22:20): `descent(time)` in moves.ts is, before T, `glide(far = the hold going on, low = coast of the close pose at T, share = trapeze(x, rise, fall), turn = trapeze(x, turnRise, turnFall))` with x from the segment's start to T, and after T `twin(diveTerrain(time))`, the close view's own entry (offset times g = x to the power 2.2). Three laws: the trapeze, the coast (tanh over coastFrames), the entry. The log height rate dents at T because the trapeze falls to the close pose's own rate at T and the entry then speeds up again. The rise is the mirror (`ascent`, exit offsets with g, `fromDive`).
- The report's rate is `log(prev.height / height)` per frame at mid shutter on the frame's side (tools/handover.ts 340), its mean so far over the far part only.
- Design sent to /root at 22:30 (and what was promised, see Next). E: one glide over the WHOLE segment between the hold going on and the surface view's own move carried back from its first frame at its own velocity (the coast anchored at the segment's end), one share for height, direction, attitude and field, its rate one bell with zero at both ends and flanks of bounded slope; the close view is drawn from the same eye through the inverse of the twin; entry and exit offsets, turnRise, turnFall, fromDive, coastFrames retire (fields stay in the type). F: a switch per stop `handover.scene`: "views" (today), "globe" (the globe side holds the close ground, the engine draws the orbit group alone from hold to travel, surface view through the twin, no T), "both" (the engine draws the terrain group through the twin into a layer, border by distance on the ground, depth against the far scene); one air (exposure, sky, limb, haze, Sun) as a curve over log height.
- The bell (decided, not coded): rate r(x) = P * S(x / a) on the rise flank, P on the plateau, P * S((1 - x) / b) on the fall, P = 1 / (1 - (a + b) / 2); S is the S curve with corner share j: S'(u) = min(u / j, 1, (1 - u) / j) / (1 - j). Its integral A(u): u <= j: u^3 / (6 j (1 - j)); j..1-j: j^2 / (6 (1 - j)) + (u - j) u / (2 (1 - j)); above: A(1 - j) + (u - (1 - j)) - (j^3 - (1 - u)^3) / (6 j (1 - j)); A(1) = 1/2. Share: P a A(x / a); P (a / 2 + x - a); 1 - P b A((1 - x) / b). Largest step of the rate per frame over the mean: P / ((1 - j) min(a, b) N) for N frames (a = b = 0.5, j = 0.25: 0.059 for 90 frames, 0.071 for 75).
- The step at Saturn 3832 (read, not reproduced): `rawMotion` is the largest of a 17 by 9 grid of tracked points; in the terrain view only rays under the horizon are tracked (the ground plane), points behind the eye at one end of the shutter are dropped, so the measure steps (18.5 to 21.9 px) and the share with it (0.759 to 0.640); the REFINE loop repeats it. Cure planned: track every ray always (sky rays at infinity, points held at the rim of a bounded field), and the share at a film position p as a weighted mean of the shares at fixed nodes in time under a smooth window.
- Agreed with travel_builder (capture engineer, branch agent/travel-polish): it edits engine.ts renderFrame and drawFrame (227 to 265), sampler.ts, motion.ts (fractional frames) first, commit due 23:45; I stay out of those until then and build the cap on its commit. Mine: moves.ts, handover.ts (sideAt by time, not by whole frame), the handover part of film.ts, the sample loop of engine.ts (318 to 426), place.ts, pace.ts.
- Answered: mars_place_builder (its option i, relief as displacement of the globe's own material, is right; planet.ts is globe_engineer's, put to /root; border as `border: [inner, outer]` in radii about patches.spot), neptune_builder (0 stays valid for placeFrames and skyFrames, hooks stay, `scene: "globe"`, `plan.heightAt(time)` promised), earth_builder (`handover: { scene: "globe" }`, asked for its near and far distances and its border).

### 22:50 to 23:00, the E measure as built (`src/director/pace.ts`, `tests/pace.test.ts`)

- The bell: `flank(u, j)` (S curve of a flank, slope a trapeze with corners of share j), `flankArea`, `bellPeak`, `bellRate(x, flanks)`, `bellShare(x, flanks)` (0 at the far end, 1 at the near end, no rate at either), `bellStep(flanks, frames)` = P / ((1 - j) min(rise, fall) frames), `checkFlanks`. Flanks = { rise, fall, corner } as shares of the segment.
- `paceLimits()` reads `config.raw.smooth` (harness_builder's block; not on main at 22:50) and falls back to the numbers of MAIL 369 (maxStep 0.08, maxRebound 0.03, seamFrames 2, ringPx 96, floors 0.05 deg and 0.5 px, rate 2, reachFrames 12). The fallback goes when the block is on main.
- `paceOf(film, plan, kind, rate = limits.rate)`: the harness's method on the film's own camera (`carried(film, plan, frame, time)`: the shot out of its set, a terrain view through `plan.twin`), capture frames k with time (k + shutterMid()) / (fps * rate), three curves per film frame (fall = sign * log(prev height / height) * rate; turn in degrees; speed = mean px of 8 ground points on a ring of ringPx round the place), the mean over the capture steps inside the segment, step = |o(k) - o(k - rate)|, half step = |o(k) - o(k - 1)|, judged across `seamFrames` on both seams, peak inside the segment, climb after and fall before the peak. Returns `{ curves: { fall, turn, speed }, ok, faults }`. It is a cross check and the plan check of my tests; THE JUDGE IS `npm run smooth`.
- Cross check against the harness's numbers of MAIL 369 (worst whole step of the rate): mine sun 0.129; mercury 0.236, 0.245; venus 0.165, 0.217; earth 0.122, 0.123; mars 0.290, 0.228; jupiter 0.178, 0.560; saturn 0.092, 0.151; uranus 0.091, 0.109; neptune 0.107, 0.174. ### 00:08 to 00:17, the speed of the picture on E, tried in the scratch tree (nothing committed)

### 00:17 to 00:40, MAIL 411 ruling 1: the near bodies in the sample count

- The ruling: a stop may name its near bodies to the motion measure, the count follows the fastest named body in the frame; its own commit on 6d260fb, nothing else in it; the count a pure function of frame, format, tier and config; a stop that names none keeps every byte; the ceiling a number of the config; at the capture rate 2 the count follows the half shutter of each capture frame. Not under the 14 px cap.
- As built: `NearBody { centre, radius }` and `SetState.near` in `src/core/types.ts`; `MoveParams.near: NearParams[]` (`centre`, `radius`, `degPerSecond`) and `StopPlan.nearBodies(time)` in `src/director/moves.ts` (the centre turned about the stop's axis by `degPerSecond ?? moonDegPerSecond` times the time since the middle of the hold: the rule Mars's set.ts moves its moon by; a stop may give the method itself); `film.ts` puts the bodies into the globe side's set state only where a stop names any; `src/core/motion.ts` `nearMotion(open, mid, close)` (the centre and four rim points, counted when the body's sphere reaches into the frame at the opening, the middle or the closing; a point that passes the camera's plane counts as the master's diagonal) and `framePlan` takes `samplesNeeded(base, cap, max(seen, near))`; `FramePlan.near` is there only in a frame with a body in it. The ceiling is the tier's `maxSamplesPerFrame` (draft 1, review 128, final 256). The method could not be called `near`: Mercury's plan class has a private field of that name.
- Mars's naming is not in my commit. The patch for mars_place_builder (3 lines in `src/stops/mars/plan.ts`): `import phobos from './phobos.json'` and `near: [{ centre: phobos.center as [number, number, number], radius: phobos.radius * phobos.hi }]`.
- With that naming, frames whose count changes. Rate 1, draft: none. Review, 34: 2218 to 2226 and 2231 to 2245 (the travel in), 2403 to 2412 (the dive). Final, 19: 2233 to 2241, 2406 to 2412, 2764 to 2766 (the travel out). 2411: review 21 to 128, final 64 to 256 (the moon's smear 245.8 px). Rate 2, review, 63 capture frames: 2219.5 to 2226.5, 2231 to 2243.5, 2404.5 to 2412, 2764 to 2766.5; final, 14: 2408.5 to 2412, 2764 to 2766.5. At rate 2 the frame 2411 gets 128 at review and 192 at final (114.7 px across its half shutter), 2411 + 1/2 gets 128 and 252 (151.2 px). No other field of any frame plan changes (raw, seen, shutter, soft, split), in all 5400 frames and all 10800 capture frames.

Where the surface view comes nearer to its place at the seam and the rise leaves it, the flow at the place turns round, and a carry that ends where the two speeds meet ends where they cancel most (Mars's rise turned red with it: falls 0.048). The Venus margin is in the record (d176). Next: the lift as its own commit with the frames that change, then the builders' lines and sets. harness_builder's probe key is ruled out.
- THE LIFT, as built. `MoveParams.light.skyLift?: { heights?: [number, number]; power?: number }`; `StopPlan.skyLift(height)` = `1 - (1 - smooth(ramp(ln from, ln to, ln height)))^power`; private `liftSpan()` reads the two heights once: the stop's own, or by default the highest height of its surface view and the lowest of its hold (sampled with `eyeHeight` at the borders of the film frames); the power is the stop's or `handover.skyLift.power` of the config (1). It refuses heights out of order ("heights of the star lift"), a power not above 0 ("power of the star lift") and an upper height over the height the rise ends at ("rise ends at": the stars would step where the open flight takes over). In `FilmDirector.shotAt` the block stands after the two views: `lifted = segment.kind === 'rise' && index > 0 ? riseLift(p.id) : 0`; where the plan gives no time (`skyLiftAt` null) `skyStars = lifted * p.skyLift(heightOver(s, pose))` in BOTH views and `light.sky = mix(light.sky, openExposure, skyStars)`; where it gives a time (Saturn, Uranus) the old course by the clock runs in the globe view alone, byte for byte.
- Why the level is 0 in a hold, a dive and a surface view: the hold wears the stop's own sky and holds keep bytes, so one function of height that lifted in the dive too would have to lie wholly above the hold (Saturn's does: 2.04 to 2.92 radii over the ring plane). The function itself takes a height and nothing else; the level is the sky the segment ends in.

- `tools/seam.ts` (committed with this line): `npx tsx tools/seam.ts [--stop ID] [--kind dive|rise] [--rate N] [--frames N] [--before N] [--rows] [--force]`. It reads the camera the film draws (`carried()` of pace.ts) in the ground's own frame (back through the stop's twin at its own time, out through the twin of one fixed time, so Mercury's rested spin and Saturn's fixed frame are read right) and prints for a seam: the surface view's own speed over its height (mean of its 2 film frames at the seam), the lowest of the move's 16 frames next to the seam over that, where the eye's way first goes against the surface view's way and how fast it is there, the largest step inside the surface view's shown frames, the frame of the change of view. `--rows` gives every capture frame with the parts along, up and across.
- A FAULT IN MY OWN NUMBERS (found 04:52): `dent.ts` read `plan.eye(kind, t, 'terrain')`, which on a stop's own path is not the drawn camera on the globe side of T. ### 05:02 to 05:35 (24 Nov), the bend: an engine option off by default, the seam tool's advice, Mars bends on its own

- WHAT THE DENT IS, in one line: the surface view ends pushing forward and down; the one move alone leaves the seam back and up (on main's Mars 161 degrees from the view's way, on Mars's own tree 169), so the eye stops and turns round. The cure is a surface view that ends on a way the move can take without a stop.
- THE ENGINE OPTION (in the commit that holds this line, off by default, no stop of the film names it): `src/director/bend.ts`, class `Bend(start, shape)`: from where it begins the eye keeps the share of its height it flies a second (so the near ground never slows) and the direction of its way turns in one plane, from its own way toward `acrossDeg` ([azimuth, elevation]: azimuth in the ground's level plane from the level part of the way, 90 to the right, 270 to the left; elevation above it) by `turnDeg`, on a smooth step (rest to rest, so no corner at the start and a straight line at the end). The place is a sum by a fourth order step at fixed nodes (`handover.carry.stepsPerFrame` a film frame), so the eye at a time does not depend on what was asked before. Nothing in it knows a frame number. `MoveParams.terrain.bend = { frames, acrossDeg, turnDeg }` puts it on the last `frames` film frames of the engine's surface view (the lens stays the straight path's own); a stop with a path of its own can build a `Bend` itself (`plan.closeGround(position, time)` gives the ground's up and the height in the close view's frame, through the twin and back).
- `plan.eyeFirstWay(kind)`: the way the one move alone takes the eye from the seam with the surface view at rest (unit vector, close view's frame). `tools/seam.ts` prints under a rise on the one move where the move alone leaves the seam and the `acrossDeg`, `turnDeg` of the bend that ends on it. `eyeOrbit` was split for it (`eyeGlide`, same operations in the same order).
- MEASURED on main's Mars with the option set in memory (scratch `out/work/tmp/bendcheck.ts`; 60 frames): acrossDeg [70.1, 45.3] turnDeg 161.2 (the plane the tool names): lowest 1.050 at 2670, never against the way, the view's largest step 0.006, judge green. [90, 0] 161.2: 1.051, green. [90, 0] 180: 1.050. [270, 0] 161.2: 1.044. 45 frames 1.046, 30 frames 1.042. On Mars's tree of 04:37 (scratch patch): the tool names [77.5, 32.8] 169.4; [90, 0] 170: 1.071, green; [270, 0] 170: 1.068, green. Without the bend 0.351 (main) and 0.225 (that tree).
- Facts of the bend: speed over height is held at 0.01102 a frame from 2610 (the straight path grows to 0.016 by 2670); the height at the seam is 1.198e-3 radii against 7.251e-4; the eye ends 1.03 straight heights from the straight end.
- PICTURES (draft, Mars's tree of 04:37 with `bend: { frames: 60, acrossDeg: [90, 0], turnDeg: 170 }` set by a scratch edit, reverted; 2606 to 2686 one by one, sheets `after-0..8.png`, without the bend 2660 to 2686 `before-0..2.png`): through the surface view no frame stands out. WHAT IT COSTS: the eye no longer stops, so the rise is further along at every frame before T: at 2686 the cliff has 55 percent of its size at 2678 (85 percent without the bend), and from 2682 to 2686 the border of the close set stands in the frame (a pale wedge at the lower right, at 2686 also along the bottom). Without the bend the ground fills the frame to 2686. So a stop that bends must look at its last close frames before T: either the close set reaches further or T comes earlier (`at`).
- tests/bend.test.ts (11): the bend of a path (no corner at its start, speed over height held, the turn in one plane from rest to rest, the track's directions, the eye at a time whatever was asked before, refusals); the bend of a stop's surface view on the covered plan (nothing before it and never the lens changes; `closeGround`; the dent 0.351 with a turn back at 2672 becomes at least 1.047 with no turn back for a track to the right, to the left and climbing; a bend that ends on `eyeFirstWay` meets it; refusals by the stop's name). Proof: 409 of 409 in 43 files, tsc clean, build exit 0, on the tree that is main 50146244 plus this commit.
- MARS BENDS ON ITS OWN (mail 05:21, agent/mars-place dd1ddb9c = its 76ab10f8 on main 50146244): `class MarsPlan` in its plan.ts, `bend.json` (fromFrame 2611, turnDeg 105, across +x: the velocity turns on a smoother step, the view's own speed law kept, Simpson's rule). ### 05:45 to 05:56 (24 Nov), THE PLAN CHECK IS THE JUDGE'S OWN (MAIL 539 point 5, own commit)

- WHAT WAS WRONG: the engine had a measure of its own for a plan (`paceOf` in `src/director/pace.ts`, used by `tools/law.ts` and two test files, never on a pixel path). Against the judge's functions (`tools/smooth/camera.ts` sampleWindow and curves, `tools/smooth/path.ts` judgeCurve) it differed in three things: (1) the speed of the picture was the mean over whatever ring points had ground in a frame (at least 4 of 8), so it stepped where their count changed; the judge compares one fixed set (the points both frames have, at least 5). (2) it took the globe for the ground of a place on the rings (Saturn); the judge takes the plane of the rings. (3) it did not set the capture rate the judge sets and asked for the whole frame's camera at a half frame; the lens at the change of view reads a hair otherwise (fourth decimal of the turn).
- MEASURED, old measure against the judge (scratch `out/work/tmp/mirror.ts`, env `FORCE=1` for the one move; main 3060b725; worst step of the speed over one film frame unless said). THE FILM AS IT FLIES: 11 of the 17 moves the same to four decimals (sun rise, mercury, venus, earth and mars both, jupiter dive, neptune dive). uranus dive 0.159 against 0.092 (half step at rate 2 0.140 against 0.046); uranus rise 0.0905 against 0.0789 (old red, judge green); neptune rise half step 0.0786 against 0.0683, turn 0.2317 against 0.2316; saturn dive mean 4.79 against 5.67 px, step 0.066 against 0.089, climb after the peak 0 against 0.045 (old green, judge red); saturn rise mean 15.85 against 13.36, step 0.074 against 0.099, fall before the peak 0 against 0.127 (old green, judge red); jupiter rise at rate 2 the fall before the peak of the rate 0.4277 against 0.4266 and of the turn 1.1395 against 1.1416. - THE CURE: `tools/lib/pace.ts`, `paceOf(film, plan, kind, rate)`: the judge's own functions on the film's camera (sampleWindow, curves with the ring of 8 in the picture and `ringPlane(plan)`, judgeCurve with the points for the speed), the capture state put back as found. Its curves are the judge's `CurveResult` (marks carry `k`; `points` for the speed). `src/director/pace.ts` keeps the bell and `paceLimits` (141 lines); `paceOf`, its `judge`, `carried`, `project`, `sphereHit` and the types are gone from src. `tools/seam.ts` reads the eye with the judge's `carriedCamera` at the capture rate (its summary lines unchanged on every stop, forced and not; in the rows only the turn at the change of view moves in the fourth decimal: earth 2100, neptune 4757 + 1/2 to 4758 + 1/2 and 4956). `tools/law.ts` searches on it: step is the worst of the whole and the half step, a `mean` fault refuses the set, `--rate N` above 1 reads a set at whole frames and at N as a build is judged, the line shows the judge's count of faults and the steps of the speed not measured. The draft tier's one sample takes the close side in frame T, in a dive and in a rise. So the T frame of the film is a mix of the two sides, unseen where they are equal and a double exposure where they differ in shape (where they differ in level only, it is the level between). `dissolveFrames` is per stop and per move (`{...config.raw.handover[kind], ...own[kind]}`), so a cut (every sample of frame T on the close side, as the draft tier draws it) could be a stop's own choice; it needs the checks in `src/config.ts` 124 and `src/director/timeline.ts` 54 opened to 0 and one line in `sideAt`. Not built (nobody asked); the look of Mars's frame 2477 at the review tier is being drawn to see what the mix does there.

WHAT MY RISE GIVES AT ITS END: the place `riseEnd` (gap under 1e-6 radii); any stated speed above the drift along its own line; it brakes or coasts at the end and does not speed up; the law does not switch within the last 1/300 s; the turn at rest; vfov as today; `eyeShot('rise', t, 'orbit')` valid up to the end. - READ for the plan (`src/director/moves.ts`): the far end of the one move in a rise is `after(time)` = `exit(time - rise.end)` = `riseEnd` carried along `riseOut` at `rise.driftSpeed`, and the bell's rate is 0 at the far end, so the eye ends at the drift alone. The eye's place is `centre + out * range(h)`, with out the direction from the place to the eye (the turn bell) and ln(h / lift^p) on the pace bell: a pace bell that keeps a rate at its far end moves the eye along the line from the place through the eye, and the camera (`lookAt(position, centre, ahead)` times the held offset) does not turn while the turn bell is at rest.

### 10:00 to 10:20 (24 Nov), 2(b) AS BUILT: the engine's sky weight and place weight by the height of the eye

- WHAT IT IS (`src/director/handover.ts`): `skyWeight(seg, time)` and `placeWeight(seg, time)` keep their signatures (Earth's set calls `skyWeight` and divides it out against its own law). Between the far end of a span and T each is `smooth(base + (1 - base) * (ln far - ln h) / (ln far - ln near))`: h the height of the globe camera over the unit sphere at the sample's time, far the height at the time the span begins (sky: `full` less `skyFrames / fps` in a dive, plus it in a rise, cut to the segment; picture: `placeFirstFrame / fps` in a dive, `(placeLastFrame + 1) / fps` in a rise), near the height at `full` (`firstFrame / fps` in a dive, `(lastFrame + 1) / fps` in a rise), base the clock's value at the far end (0, or what the clock had where the segment cuts the span: Earth, 0.166 and 0.34). THE COST AND THE CACHE: built first with no cache at all (far and near asked at every call; an `eyeShot(kind, time, 'orbit')` costs 2 to 15 microseconds, `out/work/tmp/eyecost.ts`), and the first whole chain went red on it: `tests/shutter.test.ts` (the sample count under the floor of the share) ran 6.7 s against 3.2 s and hit the 5 s limit, `tests/near.test.ts` 7.1 s against 4.8 s, the frame plan test of `tests/handover.test.ts` 2.8 s against 1.2 s. Measured (`out/work/tmp/shotcost.ts`): `skyWeight` 24 microseconds and `placeWeight` 16 a call on Mars's dive, three poses each, about as much again as the rest of a shot. AS BUILT NOW: the two ends of a span are asked once for a film and kept with its eye (`spanEnds`, a WeakMap by the eye function the film registers; key: segment id, far time, near time, the forced one move, the count of handover changes), and the film asks the height of the sample once for both weights (`eyeHeightAt`, passed as the optional third argument of `skyWeight` and `placeWeight`). A film is made for the paths its plans have when it is made (its legs are read once, too), so the ends can only go stale where a test changes a path under a living film without a new plan or the forced one move; none does. The shot dumps of the cached build equal the uncached ones by sha256 at both rates (`after2-1.txt`, `after2-2.txt`). The seven tests that make the plan of every frame of every dive and rise got a limit of 60 s (`LONG` in `tests/shutter.test.ts`, `tests/handover.test.ts`, `tests/pace.test.ts`): they ran 2.5 to 3.8 s of the 5 s before this already.
- THE T SIDE keeps its gates, which are the handover itself: sky 1 from `full` on in a dive and before `full` in a rise; picture 1 inside the frame of T, 0 where the close view has the frame. THE FAR SIDE has no gate: above the far height the line runs on down to 0.
- NOT ASKED FOR A HEIGHT: a stop with its own `skyShare` (the film never calls `skyWeight` for it), `skyFrames` 0 (a step, as before), no picture (`placeWeight` is 0; it was a ramp that nothing read).
- THE EYE: `eyeHeights(of)` in handover.ts, set by the film at its construction and at every `shotAt` beside `shutterScales` (`FilmDirector.eyeOf`: the plan's `eyeShot(kind, time, 'orbit').pose.position.length() - 1`). The view is named, so it never comes back through `sideAt`. THROWS: no film has said how to ask; the eye is not over the body at T or not lower at T than where the span begins.
- STILL BY THE CLOCK, said in the NAMED mail: `skyTurnWeight` (the turn of the sky's frame from the segment's far end to T, a motion like the path's pace, not a fade).
- THE VIEW `nosky` goes in with it (`forceNoSky`, `noSkyForced`, probe key `nosky`).
- THE T LINE OF THE HANDOVER REPORT (`tools/handover.ts`, `Line.mix(tier)`, MAIL 626): the samples of frame T per side at the final, review and draft tier (`framePlan` for the count and the shutter, `samplesFor`, `sideAt`); also `samplesAtT` in the JSON.
- PROOF SO FAR. Shot dumps (`out/work/tmp/shotdump.ts`, before and after, `/tmp/law/h611/dump/`, compared with `/tmp/law/h611/cmp.py`): at rate 1, 427 of 5400 shots differ, at rate 2, 854 of 10800, all inside the sky spans (sun.rise 217..240, mercury 736..759 and 978..1001, venus 1319..1342 and 1543..1566, earth 1830..1918 and 2101..2174, mars 2451..2474 and 2688..2711, jupiter 3000..3023 and 3271..3294, neptune 4734..4757 and 4957..4980); Saturn and Uranus equal; the frame plans of all three tiers equal at every frame.
- WHAT THE LAW DOES TO THE FADES (`out/work/tmp/steps611.ts`, the largest step of a weight between two frames at mid shutter, and the frames between 0.05 and 0.95; clock against height). Sky: 0.062 (18 frames) by the clock everywhere but Earth; by height sun 0.062 (17), mercury 0.068 and 0.071 (17), venus 0.083 (14) and 0.072 (16), mars 0.073 and 0.069 (16), jupiter 0.079 (16) and 0.079 (14), neptune 0.067 and 0.064 (17); Earth 0.012 (73) to 0.016 (66) in the dive, 0.012 (58) to 0.018 (52) in the rise. Picture: 0.124 (9 frames) by the clock; by height venus 0.167 (8) and 0.143 (8), mars 0.133 (9) and 0.128 (9), jupiter 0.124 (9) and 0.138 (8), EARTH 0.265 (5) AND 0.248 (6): Earth's eye flies almost level over the last 8 frames before T (3.61e-2 radii at 1907, 3.10e-2 at 1915, 3.09e-2 at T), so the height changes only at the start of the window and the picture comes in there. With `placeFrames` 24 Earth would get 0.107 (11 frames) in the dive and 0.105 (11) in the rise (`out/work/tmp/earthspan.ts`: 18 gives 0.158 and 0.153, 30 gives 0.078 and 0.076). That value is Earth's (its place.json): said in the NAMED mail, not changed by me.

- THE FAR END OF THE RISE (MAIL 602, travel_builder's interface), DESIGNED, NOT BUILT YET. `Flanks.out` in `src/director/pace.ts`: the rate the bell keeps at its far end over its mean rate (above 0, at most 1). Plateau p = (1 - out A) / (1 - A - B), A = rise (1 - W_rise), B = fall (1 - W_fall); rate on the far flank out + (p - out) S; share out x + (p - out) rise Area. With out absent every number as today (the key is not even read). Only the height takes it: the turn and the angle of view run on `rest` (the same pace without out), so the turn is at rest at the end as today. `EyeLaw { pace, turn, liftPower, rest? }` with no `rest` key when out is absent (the onemove tests use toEqual and key lists). REFUSED: in a dive, in a rise into a hold (the Sun) and in a rise no travel follows (Neptune goes into `ending.retreat`). A travel follows the rise of mercury, venus, earth, mars, jupiter, saturn, uranus. Mars is the only stop on the one move today; all others override `risePose` and get the same from their builders. travel_builder reads p0 = `exitPose(end).position`, p1 and p2 = `eyeShot('rise', end - h, 'orbit')` and `end - 2h`, h = 1/600 s, v = (3 p0 - 4 p1 + p2) / (2h), a = (p0 - 2 p1 + p2) / (h h), excess w = v less riseOut times driftSpeed; under 0.02 radii a second its carry is off. My side owes: the place `riseEnd` (gap under 1e-6 radii), a stated speed above the drift along its own line, braking or coasting at the end and never speeding up, no switch of the law within the last 1/300 s, turn at rest, vfov as today, `eyeShot('rise', t, 'orbit')` valid up to the end for own path stops too. Prepared edit scripts (not applied; the second is untested): `/tmp/law/h602/edit_pace.py`, `/tmp/law/h602/edit_moves.py`; the math check `/tmp/law/h602/pace.ts`, `math.ts` (integral 1, rate(0) = out, bellStep = steepest slope over frames). If `/tmp` is gone the design above is enough to write it again.

My rise must give: the place `riseEnd` (gap under 1e-6 radii), any stated speed above the drift along its own line, at the end it brakes or coasts and does not speed up, the law does not switch within the last 1/300 s, the turn at rest, vfov as today, `eyeShot('rise', t, 'orbit')` valid up to the end for own path stops too.
- DESIGN A, DROPPED (`Flanks.out` in pace.ts: the bell keeps a rate at its far end; `/tmp/law/h602/A.patch`): the eye keeps the speed of the whole path at the end, so its excess stands 7.7 degrees off the drift's line and the body's centre flies 3.5 px a frame toward +45 degrees at the seam and then turns to -156 through 0.91 px: a reversal at speed, a bend a viewer sees.
- DESIGN B, AS BUILT: THE FAR END COMES OUT. `handover.rise.pullBack: { speed, over }` in a stop's own params (`HandoverSide.pullBack` in handover.ts; speed in radii a second above the drift at the end of the rise, over in seconds). `StopPlan.pullBack(time)` in moves.ts: how far the far end of the rise stands back along the line of the exit (`riseOut`), in radii: s = min((rise.end - time) / over, 1), back = speed over (s - s^3 + s^4 / 2). So before the last `over` seconds the far end stands back by speed over / 2 and drifts as before; in the last `over` seconds its excess over the drift comes in as the smooth step 1 - 3 s^2 + 2 s^3 (no step in speed or acceleration where it leaves the drift, acceleration 0 at the end), and it reaches the end pose at drift + speed. `after(time)` is `exitPose(time)` less that (the hold where the rise goes into one). The rise comes to rest on its far end as it always did, so the eye ends on `riseEnd` and flies drift + speed along the drift's own line; the turn and the angle of view are the end pose's, untouched. Every path that glides onto `after(time)` gets it, the one move and a stop's own alike. WITHOUT THE OPTION `pullBack` returns 0 and `after` does not touch the pose: to the bit. REFUSED (throws at the first call): a rise no travel follows (the Sun goes into its hold, Neptune into the retreat), speed or over not above 0 or not finite, over longer than the rise, over shorter than a film frame, a far end that would stand in the body where the rise begins.

- THE BODY STEP IN PLACE, why: jupiter_coder measured (mail 12:40) that my body measure (`tools/handover.ts`, "the mean level of the body steps", limit `handover.limits.levelStep` 6) reads the frame opening on the dark rim and the night side while the eye climbs, not a change of the look of any ground, and that to hold my number it carries a camera exposure by height that costs it limb rows and pop. The frames part of my report is a measure of every stop's round (`kit/PROGRESS.md` line 1037). A measure that reads the framing as a cut pushes builders to bend the picture: sound, taken.
- WHAT IT IS: a pixel of a frame is laid over the frame before by the ground it shows (its ray to the unit sphere of the body, back by this frame's turn of the body, forward by the turn of the frame before, projected into that frame, read between its pixels). Ground the frame before does not show is NEW ground: counted as a share, left out of the change. The rim of the body, one pixel in either frame, holds sky and is left out (without that a synthetic rise read +0.80 where the truth is 0). A frame gets: `seen` (share of the body whose ground the frame before shows), `level` and `colour` (mean change on it, signed), `rms`, `fresh` (level of the new ground), `kept` (level of the ground seen in both).
- THE CONVENTION, proven on real pictures (`/tmp/law/ip/proto.py`): ground = turn(now) inverse times the point, then turn(before). Close view of Jupiter 3241 to 3268: rms 4 to 5 that way, 33 to 45 without the turn or with it reversed.
- STATE OF THE CODE (13:25): written and run, NOT committed, taken out of the tree for the merge. Saved as `out/work/inplace/keep/` (the four files and `inplace.patch` against fd96f0ff; the same in `/tmp/law/ip/keep/`): `tools/img/handover.py` (new op `inPlace`: `turned`, `back`, `small`, `ground`, `onto`, `taken`, `inner`, `in_place`; the op `frames`, which the judge's place part in `tools/smooth.ts` calls, untouched), `tools/handover.ts` (rows carry `inPlace`; one more line in the report, "The same step in place, on the ground both frames show (shown, not judged)"; five columns at the END of the table; `judged.inPlace` in the JSON; two pictures a segment, `NAME-KIND-step-body.png` and `-step-in-place.png`: frame before, frame, new ground in magenta, change in place times `differenceGain`), `tests/inplace.test.ts` (5 tests on pictures painted by three's own camera: a frame that pulls back onto a dark cap steps the body's mean by 34.5 and reads 0.02 in place; the look lifted by 10 reads 8.26 where 8.28 is the truth; a dive reads seen 1.0 and 0.02; a turn that is not told reads rms 13.5 against 2.7; the picture of a pair), `config/showcase.json` (two keys in `handover.limits`).

- THE PATTERN MEASURE AS IT IS ON ed003c70 (read, not run): `pictures` works at a quarter of the size; for each frame i and `changeFrames` = reach, `change` chains the flows i to i + reach (DIS, forward and back, `along` drops places where the two do not agree), carries frame i + reach BACK onto the grid of frame i (`moved`), judged only where the chain starts on the body and arrives on it (`body(pose, ...)` of both frames: the tool holds the poses). `pattern(a, moved, ok, fine, wide, floor)`: detail between two Gaussian widths (wide = `patternPx` 40 times 0.25 = 10 quarter px, fine = wide / 5 = 2), each divided by its local contrast, 1 less their correlation over the wide window; judged where half of the wide window is judged ground and both have detail over the floor; `patterned` gives the 99th percentile, and None where under 2 percent of the frame is judged (`PATTERN_SHARE`). In a rise the later frame is smaller, so carried back it is enlarged z times and holds no detail under about z quarter px: that is globe_engineer's false red.

Offered: when the patch is here, an exchange planted in a rigid rise, read with and without the correction. - NOT SAID IN THE MAIL, my own reckoning (not measured): a linear dissolve between two unrelated patterns of equal contrast reads 1 - 2uv / (u u + v v) in a window that sees the shares u and v of it; over 8 frames of a 16 frame dissolve that is 0.40 (under 0.45), and the limit is reached only by dissolves shorter than about 15 frames. And in a rise that shrinks 3 times in 8 frames a ground detail stays between the two widths (a factor 5) for about 12 frames, so the windows that can still show a fine detail end inside a longer exchange and see it half done (about 0.29). To measure when the patch is here.

- THE CURE of the pattern measure. Not named to me by mail yet; found on `agent/engine-globe`: 23a13bae3f1f5db18fb8dd84b944b7dc3fa9932b (18:13, "The pattern measure keeps its band in a rise: the farther frame carried from its finer picture, the way set right, a dive read on its nearer frame too") on eb2975a6 (the first patch, not taken by d236); head 780020f6 (two notebook commits and the merge of main dfdc58cf). `tools/img/smooth.py` of the head is byte equal to 23a13bae's (saved as `/tmp/law/cure/smooth.py`, `made.py` beside it, its notebook as `/tmp/law/cure/GLOBE-NOTES.md`, section 12).
- What the cure does (READ in its text): the widths stay 8 and 40 px. `carry` gives the map of the chain (or the chain run back); `enlarged` reads from the slopes of the map how many times the carried frame is enlarged; `sharper` carries the farther frame again from a finer picture (the quarter size times 2 to the power of half steps, the full size at most) where it is enlarged by 1.19 or more and sets the map right with three rounds of a flow at the finest scale smoothed over 5 quarter px; `readings` reads a pair onward and, where the later frame is the nearer one (a dive), a second time from the other end; `reading` takes the larger; `band` names the width under which the window read nothing (8 px to an enlargement of 4, 2 px times the enlargement beyond). `pictures()` gives `patternBand`.
- `rig3.py` (scratch, `/tmp/law/pat/send/rig3.py`, NOT on my branch): the rig's rigid rises and dives read three ways: as on main (`RIG_SMOOTH`, the repo's file, which is main's), by the cure's own calls as its pictures part makes them (`RIG_CURE=/tmp/law/cure/smooth.py`: carry, change, readings, reading; full size frames handed over as float64 as its `grey` gives them), and with the map known (the nearer frame against the farther one carried by the true map from its finer picture, the widths times z / 4 beyond 4). One JSON line a case in `/tmp/law/cure/rig3.jsonl`; batch `/tmp/law/cure/batch.sh LIST` (lists: 50 rises, 50 dives, 30 more halves at 32 and 64 px), log `/tmp/law/cure/batch.log`, started 18:42:23 niced beside the render, about 10 s a case.
- Its unit tests: `python3 tools/smooth/made.py carried` runs from a scratch tree `/tmp/law/cure/tree/` after the batch (`/tmp/law/cure/made.sh`, log `made.log`), checked by hand against the eleven expectations of its `tests/smooth.test.ts` with `/tmp/law/cure/checkmade.py`.

- The old law (travel_builder's 61474ebd, on main as 6bc27a49): `sunSeen = max(near(time) - near(seg.start), 0)`, `near = ramp(-16, 14, degrees the Sun stands inside the frame's edge)`, `light.sky = mix(light.sky, openExposure, open); light.sky *= 1 - 0.25 * smooth(sunSeen) * (1 - smoother(ramp(0.8, 1, u)))`. So the dim CAME by the Sun's angle and WENT by the leg's clock; its window [-16, 14] was fitted by travel_builder on the old pose so that the level never fell (its notes, line 974 on).
- The dim acted on ONE leg of the film: uranus.travel, where the Sun comes in to stay (5.0 degrees inside the frame's edge in Neptune's hold). Sun inside the edge at the start | the end | deepest: mercury 1.69 | -66.87 | 1.76 (dim 4e-6); venus -64.63 | -57.71; earth -57.71 | -48.88; mars -45.92 | -71.08; jupiter -51.03 | -41.14; saturn -53.00 | -57.57; uranus -50.03 | 5.05 on the tip's pose (turn 61.6 degrees), -56.90 | 5.02 on the new pose.
- WHY NO DIM THAT FOLLOWS A STAYING SUN CAN HOLD THE TEST AT EVERY RATE: the level is F(opening, Sun's depth); the opening runs on the leg's clock (travel.sky, not mine); if the Sun's coming lowers the level at all, a turn fast enough makes the level fall. The only laws that hold "never falls" at every turn rate are those in which a Sun that comes in to stay lowers nothing.
- THE LAW TAKEN (src/director/film.ts): `SUN_DIM = { level: 0.25, nearDeg: [-16, 14] }` (the clock span `back` is gone); `sunSeen = max(near(time) - max(near(seg.start), near(seg.end)), 0)`: only what lies deeper than at BOTH ends of the leg; `dim = index > 0 ? level * smooth(sunSeen) : 0; light.sky = mix(light.sky, openExposure * (1 - dim), open)`: the dim lowers the open flight's share only, never a stop's own sky. A Sun that comes in to stay or leaves for good dims nothing; a Sun that passes through dims by its depth and gives it back by the same depth. In the film as it stands the dim is under 5 millionths on every leg.
- The alternative NOT taken (the dim kept on a staying Sun, on the open share, reference the start only): 4509 0.5012, 4512 0.5166, 4515 0.5395, 4518 0.5776, 4521 0.6148, 4524 0.6409, 4527 0.65 by hand on the new pose; monotone at the film's rate, but only to about twice that turn rate.

  - THE CAUSE AS READ (not proven until the candidate's picture is seen): `setSamples(count, GRAD_FLOOR, soft)` (`src/diagnostics/diag.ts`; `GRAD_FLOOR = 0.5` in `src/core/engine.ts`) reads every lookup through `uGrad` at 1 / sqrt(count) of the pixel, down to a half from 4 samples on, on the assumption that the samples of a frame lie over one another and add up to the pixel. Outside a dive or a rise the softening `motion.softFromPx` 10 to `softToPx` 18 takes that back as the frame moves (at 14.3 px: 0.77 of a pixel). In a dive or a rise that hands over `framePlan` returns `soft: 0`, so a frame of 24 samples spread over 14.3 px reads at half a pixel with 1.7 samples to a pixel of the path: a light smaller than a pixel is met by one sample and missed by the next.

The engine gives two uniforms: `uLawFocal` = the focal length in px of the 1080 master of the lens of the pass that is drawn (`view.format.height / (2 * frameUniform.value * (view.placeScale ?? 1) * tan(vfov / 2))`, so 540 / tan(vfov / 2) at the master, the same in a place picture) and `uLawFrame` (buffer px a master px takes in this pass). Rules that throw: one call only; every uniform of the text is named `uLaw` + own name and is given, and nothing is given that the text does not declare; the text has no `void main`, `discard`, `outColor`, `gl_FragDepth`. A relief with a law is NO SHIELD (what lies behind is drawn) unless `whole()` answers exactly true (the set stands for it that the law gives exactly 1 then; Mars: `whole: () => uLawAir.value.z < 0.5`).
    - `tone: true` (ask of 02:23): the text also defines `vec3 lawTone(vec3 P, vec3 E, vec2 image)`, a factor on the layer's linear colour on the line `vec3 col = inverseFilmic(texel.rgb) * uGain * vig;`, before the valley mist and before the share, held at or above 0.     - `presenceDetail([from, to] | null, { size })`: a share by the size a texel of the picture takes on the screen (the Jacobian of the picture's place against the pixel). THE FACET FINDING: this measure is exact only where triangles stand across the view: 19 percent off at a corner of a 4 cell test mesh under a 40 degree lens, about 2 percent at 48 cells; the rasteriser's vertex snapping reads up to 0.3 percent off on an 8 px cell (the GL test reads pixel 0,0 with a tolerance of 0.02 on the share). This is why Mars went to a law of its own (way (b)): its law counts by the eye's distance and the texel, no derivative.
    - Mars's law (its `src/stops/mars/law.ts`, numbers in its `close.json`): uniforms `uLawTexel` vec4, `uLawAir` vec4, `uLawEye` vec3; helpers `lawSine`, `lawLevel`; reads `uUpL`, `uLawFocal`. With `uLawAir.z` at 0 it returns exactly 1.0 (the surface view), and 2580 must keep its bytes there. Its text is one of my GL tests (compiles in the layer shader; one pixel a texel from the picture's own eye; bit equal with the switch off).
