  - `gradAt` AS BUILT: 09cfadcd "Engine: the footprint of a lookup by the way of its own point (gradAt)", one commit on 7e3da15a, tree `step-wt`, branch `agent/engine-way`. No config change and no shader calls it: no pixel should move.
    - `src/diagnostics/diag.ts`: `wayUniform` (4 floats: samples, cover, floor, soft), `wayOpenUniform`, `wayCloseUniform` (16 floats each), `gradUniforms = { uGrad, uWay, uWayOpen, uWayClose }`, `interface Way { cover; open; close }`, `setWay(way, samples = 0, floor = 0, soft = 0)` (off, `uWay[0] = 0`: without a way, at 1 sample or less, at cover 0), `GRAD_GLSL` (declares the four uniforms and `float gradAt(vec3 view)`: off it returns `uGrad`; way = the distance of the two projections `xy / w`, 1e9 where w is not above 1e-9 at either end; count = min(uWay.x / (max(way, 1.0) * uWay.y), uWay.x); sharp = max(inversesqrt(max(count, 1.0)), uWay.z); returns sharp + (1.0 - sharp) * uWay.w).
    - `src/core/motion.ts`: `FramePlan.cover?` (there with `sharp`); `wayMatrix(shot, state, end, at, share = 1)`: the view space of the sample to (x px, y px, unused, 1) times the distance in front of the end's lens, the point fixed in the set's frame and turned with `state.turn`, composed in doubles; `frameWay(source, frame, plan, mid)`: null without a cover or at one sample, else a function of a sample's shot that gives `{ cover, open, close }` from the shots at `shutterOffset(0, plan.shutter)` and `shutterOffset(1, plan.shutter)` on the sample's own side (the side is forced only in a split frame), share = split ? 1 : seenShare(mid); null for a shot that is not one set of the same id and side at both ends.

- WHAT IT SAYS. (1) No single `terrain.ground` cures a stop: the two borders ask for two values in every stop (0.57 at Jupiter and 0.39 at Earth, which I gave /root at 09:25, are the rise borders alone), and inside the holds the ratio of the true reading to the plane's is no constant either (Mars 1.18 to 1.90, Mercury 1.47 to 2.55, Neptune 2.15 to 2.77, Venus 1.31 to 1.79, Earth 1.74 to 2.21, Jupiter 0.81 to 2.14, Uranus 1.12 to 2.26, Sun 0.80 to 1.04). (2) `terrain.ground` (getter `moves.ts` 297) has two readers: the measure (`film.ts` 710 into SetState.ground, read at `src/core/motion.ts` 58) and the default twin (`moves.ts` 408, 411: `twinFrame`, also read by Mars's `set.ts` 313). ONLY MARS uses the default twin: all eight others override `twin` (sun 190, mercury 179, venus 111, earth 114, jupiter 265, saturn 337 with `override`; uranus 207 and neptune 129 without the keyword). Measured with `q4g.ts`: with 0.741 the globe side's lens of Mars moves (0.0046 radii at 2440, 0.0008 at 2460; it stands 0.00173 radii over the sphere at 2477 for 0.00162 now); in the other eight the lens does not move at all. (3) The cause is the shape: the flat plane lies across the set's y axis; the ground as the twin has it is tilted against that axis by 18 to 33 degrees in the 17 frames (Saturn 66 to 72) and is a ball.
- THE ONE LAW (the model `q4h.ts`, "fixed"): the surface view's measure takes its points on the globe as the plan's twin places it in the set: the unit sphere brought into the set by the twin undone at the middle of the shutter (`zero = twin(origin pose)`, `unit = twin((1,0,0))`: centre `(0 - zero.position)` turned by the inverse of `zero.quaternion` over the scale, radius 1 over the scale), its points fixed in the set as the plane's are today. It reads the globe side's number at both borders of seven stops (Sun, Venus, Earth to 0.003 px, Mars, Jupiter, Uranus, Neptune) with no value in any plan. It is equal to the unit sphere measured through the twin with the body's turn ("turned") wherever the twin turns with `turn(time)`.
- MERCURY'S GLOBE TURN (found 09:40, by code and by the model, NOT by a picture): Mercury's set paints the globe by `spin(time) = plan.spin(time) - spinRate * spinRest * plan.rested(time)` (`set.ts` 122, 393; the near patch by `plan.drawn(state.time)`, 316; `look.ts` spinRest 1, flight.rest [744, 990], knee 30 frames), so the drawn body comes to rest between 714 and 774 and starts again between 960 and 1020. The engine's state says `turn: p.turn(time)` (`film.ts` 710), the full spin. SetState.turn is read only by the measure (`src/core/motion.ts` 24, 44, 55, 429, 430: rawMotion and wayMatrix). So Mercury's globe side is misread in every plan today: dive 714 to T (62.31 for 64.07 at 744, 53.28 for 56.43 at 750, 35.66 for 41.78 at 759, 33.51 for 40.01 at 760), rise T to 1020 (58.22 for 52.86 at 977, 64.91 for 60.67 at 980, 85.71 for 84.22 at 990, equal from 1020). All of it above 14 px, so it does not move the share of the shutter law of commit 2, but it moves counts. With `drawn(time)` in the state the globe side reads 40.0123 at 760 and 52.8558 at 977, the law's numbers to four places. Cure by cause: the state carries the turn the stop draws (a base `drawn(time)` in `StopPlan` that returns `turn(time)`; Mercury's plan has `drawn` already; tsconfig has no noImplicitOverride, so no file of Mercury changes).
- SATURN stands apart: its surface view is the ring plane (ground 0 named), so the plane is the right shape there and the twin's ball is not (its twin does not turn with the body either: "fixed" reads 1.98 and 11.37 where the globe side reads 2.94 and 17.80). Its dive border reads 2.94 on the globe side and 8.80 on the surface side; which is true is the owner's to say. Not to be named in the law.
- WHAT THE LAW MOVES where a stop is named: surface views read more. Largest in the hold, plane to twin: Mercury 3.91 to 9.88, Venus 2.91 to 4.64, Earth 1.30 to 2.70, Mars 4.50 to 8.34, Jupiter 4.04 to 6.77, Uranus 9.64 to 11.02 (a steady 10.9 through the hold), Neptune 1.04 to 2.23; the Sun less, 8.85 to 7.08. So counts in holds rise, and Mars's hold passes 6 px near its end (5.51 at 2580, 7.87 at 2668, 8.06 at 2670), where the shutter law 6 to 14 then gives a small share inside the hold: "border frames equal by sha" of commit 3 holds only on a tree where Mars is not named in commit 4.
- VERIFIED BY PICTURE only at Jupiter 3240 and Earth 2100 (flow fits the globe side's measure, before 09:25). Everything else here is computed from the plans.
- MAIL TO /root 09:45 UTC (`/tmp/law/mail-q4.txt`): hold the ground values; the table; the two more reasons (Mars's twin, the shape); one law, yes, by the twin; Mercury's globe turn; Saturn apart; what the law moves; the plan for commit 4: a switch `handover.drawnMeasure`, a list of stops, empty by default: for a named stop the surface view is measured on the twin's globe and the globe side by the turn the plan draws; wayMatrix follows by the state's turn and needs nothing in the surface view (the shader gives it the true point). ## E as built (23:45), the engine's one move

- Switches: `handover.ownPath` (my config block): the stops that keep the path of their own class. Today all but mars. A builder takes its name off in its own commit. `--diag onemove` (probe.ts, `forceOneMove`) draws any stop on E without touching the config; the scene word carries `|onemove` so the caches do not mix.
- `StopPlan.engineMove`: hands over and (forced or not in ownPath).
- `plan.eye(kind, time, view)`: the camera in the given view (on E the one eye, in the terrain view through `untwin`; off E `divePose` and `risePose`). `plan.eyeShot(kind, time, forced?)`: with the view the film draws. `plan.eyeHeight(time)`: height above the unit sphere in radii over hold, dive, surface view and rise; the film puts it on `SetState.height`. `plan.closePose(kind, time)` on E is the one eye in the terrain view (Mars's set lays its plates through it). Sets and plans that call `plan.dive`, `plan.rise`, `divePose`, `risePose` (jupiter/pall.ts, mercury/set.ts, earth/set.ts, earth/plan.ts) must call `eye`, `eyeShot`, `eyeHeight` before they leave ownPath.
- The path: `eyeOrbit` = `glide(kind, time, far, low, share, share, seam, { swing })` with x the time share of the segment counted from the far end, `share = bellShare(x, flanks)`, far = `sidestep(hold or after)`, low = the twin of `eyeCoast` (the surface view's seam pose carried at its velocity and spin, tanh bound to coastFrames), swing constant (`eyeSwing`, 0 with `path.level` false).
- Flanks: `handover.pace` {rise 0.4, fall 0.4, corner 0.2}; per stop and side `handover.KIND.pace`. Named by end for both kinds: `rise` is the flank at the far end, `fall` the flank at the surface. `eyeFlanks` refuses flanks whose bell would step by more than `smooth.path.maxStep` (bellStep: 0.058 for 90 frames, 0.069 for 75).
- A stop on E may set: its two ends (hold, terrain, the travel's first pose), its place (spot), `at`, `path.sidestep` {right, up, by}, `path.level`, `handover.KIND.pace`. Not read on E: `terrain.entry*`, `terrain.exit*`, `path.rise`, `fall`, `turnRise`, `turnFall`, `levelBy`, `fromDive`, a class's own `divePose`, `risePose`, `dive`, `rise`.
- `paceLimits()` reads `config.raw.smooth` only (no stand in numbers any more).

## E6 as built (18:30), the capped shutter

- Numbers: `handover.maxBlurPx` (px of motion a frame of an opted in dive or rise may keep across its shutter) and `handover.blurEaseFrames` (the length of the ramp at a seam that moves more) in `config/showcase.json`; checked in `src/config.ts`. Nothing else is a number.
- `src/core/motion.ts`: `blurAllowed(source, frame)` (Infinity outside the dives and rises of stops that hand over), `shutterScale(source, frame)` (1 there; memo per source, dropped when a plan, one of the two numbers or the side the harness forces changes), `framePlan` gives `shutter`, `raw` (full shutter, as before) and `seen` (the motion across the shutter that is open; the sample count follows it). Frames outside run the old lines and get `shutter: 1`.
- `src/core/sampler.ts`: `samplesFor(count, shutter)` spreads the samples over the share around the middle; with 1 it is the old expression, to the bit; one sample is the middle whatever the share (the draft tier). `shutterOffset(position, shutter)` and `shutterPosition(offset, shutter)` go between the place 0 to 1 inside the open shutter and the time inside the frame.
- The side of a sample in the frame that swaps sides (`sideAt` in `src/director/handover.ts`) goes by the sample's place inside the shutter that is open, so the pattern of sides is the one of the full shutter. `sideAt` is called by the stop plans with a time only, so the share reaches it through a provider: `shutterScales(of)`, set by the film that was built or asked for a shot last (`src/director/film.ts`). It is asked only where the weight is between 0 and 1; the measure itself names its sides, so it never comes back to `sideAt`. In a test that calls `sideAt` on a dissolve frame, build the film first and take the sample times with `samplesFor(count, shutterScale(film, frame))`.
- `src/core/engine.ts` draws with `samplesFor(plan.samples, plan.shutter)` and hands every set `view.shutter` (`RenderView`, 1 outside the capped frames). The sky and the middle shot stay at the middle of the full shutter.
- A set that takes a time step from the shutter for a smear of its own (Mercury: `config.shutterFraction / (view.samples * config.fps)`) multiplies it by `view.shutter ?? 1`.
- The report (`npm run handover`, part `frames`, or `--only path` without pictures): per frame `shutter`, `blur centre`, `blur edge`, `blur most`, `px a frame`; three lines per segment (Shutter, Seams, Motion from frame to frame against the blur); the same in the JSON under `blur`; judged in "every frame": no frame keeps more than it may (hundredths of a px). `npm run samples -- --tier review --frames a,b` prints the share of a capped frame.
- To try another cap: change `handover.maxBlurPx` only. With 6 the review tier takes 16 samples in every capped frame (6 / 0.6 = 10, under the fast count), with 14 it takes 24.
- The floor (19:30, Mail 332): `handover.minShutter`, 0 for off, checked 0 to 1 in `src/config.ts`. In `shutterScale`, after the correction loop: a capped frame never runs with less than this share of the film's shutter, `scale = max(allowed / raw, floor)`. A frame that is not capped keeps its full shutter, so the seams and the slow frames do not change, and with 0 no value changes at all. The frames at the floor keep more than the cap (`seen` of the plan), most at the rim and next to none at the place the camera goes to; the sample count follows `seen` by the film's own rule (`motion.maxStepPx`, within `maxSamplesPerFrame` of the tier; the draft tier stays at 1 sample). The report prints the line "Floor" per segment (how many frames run at the floor, the longest blur and its frame, the most samples the review tier takes) and the column "samples at review" beside `shutter` and `blur most`; the blur verdict of "every frame" judges only the frames above the floor. To try: change `handover.maxBlurPx` and `handover.minShutter`, nothing else.

## E3 as built (11:30), the generic path of a stop that opted in

Files: `src/director/moves.ts` (`coast`, `glide`, `swing`, `unrolled`, `handoverPath`, `descent`, `ascent`), `src/director/handover.ts` (`PathOptions`, `HandoverSide.path`, `HandoverSide.skyFloor`), `src/core/skyfloor.ts`, `src/core/engine.ts` (`floored`), `src/director/film.ts` (`skyFloorOf`), `config.handover` (`descent`, `ascent`, `zenithDeg`, `coastFrames`, `skyFloor`), tests `tests/descent.test.ts`, `tests/skyfloor.test.ts`.

- Two schedules. `share` (trapeze of the time between the far end and T with `rise`, `fall`) drives log height and the field of view. `turn` (trapeze with `turnRise`, `turnFall`) drives the direction from the place and the attitude. The velocity ramps of the trapeze are smoothsteps. Generic numbers: rise 0.2, fall 0.3, turnRise 0.2, turnFall 0.6 for both kinds.
- The low end is not the twin of the close pose at the sample time but `twin(coast(kind, time))`: the close pose at T carried on with its own velocity and spin, the reach saturating over `coastFrames` (8, tanh). At T it is the twin with the close side's speed, turn rate and climb (tested to 1e-3).
- Level glide (`level: true`, the generic default). The far pose is swung about the vertical of the place into the upright plane that holds the close side's heading (`swing`), the path is flown in that plane, and the swing is given back as the camera gets far. So near the ground the camera flies straight along the heading, the horizon is level whenever it is a line in the frame, and the place picture is seen from the twin's own side.
  - Geometry: looking at the place from elevation e and off plane azimuth phi with the picture upright, the horizon tilts by atan(tan(phi) / sin(e)). Level needs phi = 0 and no twist.
  - `swing` = `At + w * wrap(Ap - At)`. `At` is the turn that leaves the far pose without twist against the look frame (`unrolled`, the first form). `Ap` brings the far position into the plane, on the side (in front of the place or behind it) that leaves the smaller twist; the side is decided once per kind at the far end and cached (`leans`). `w` is a smoothstep of the angle of the far position from the zenith of the place over `zenithDeg` ([2, 8]): a far pose that stands over the place has no plane to be told apart and keeps the first form.
  - One weight `upright = smooth(turn / levelBy)` spends both the swing (`swing * (1 - upright)`) and the twist of the far pose (`a.rel.slerp(b.rel, upright)`). Spent together they turn the picture one way only. `levelBy`: descent 0.8, ascent 0.5 (per stop: `handover.dive.path.levelBy`).
  - Mars numbers. Dive: hold pose at elevation 81.3 over the place, 121.6 degrees from behind, twist -15.4; the picture turns 105.7 degrees, at most 6.6 degrees a frame (2418), and stands still from 2431; the horizon enters near 2425 within 1 degree of level. Rise: travel pose at elevation 52, 44 degrees off the front half plane, twist -40; the camera pulls straight back and up in the twin's plane, over the top (emission angle 71 at T, 0.7 at 2726, 38 at the end); the horizon is level until it leaves the top of the frame near 2707; the picture turns by 4 degrees in all.
  - Tried and dropped: the swing spent linearly over the whole turn (the first level glide). Dive fine (a spiral, 4.3 degrees a frame at most, the horizon scrolling sideways until 2446); rise wrong: with the first swing (3.9 degrees) the horizon banked from 2695 (8 degrees at 2698, 30 at 2712) while in frame; with the in plane swing spent linearly the picture turned 23 degrees one way and 20 back.
- Old path for a stop that wants it: `handover: { dive: { path: { turnFall: 0.3, level: false } } }` (and `rise`).
- Mars plan (`src/stops/mars/plan.ts`): `rise: { at: 0.2, placeFrames: 26 }` puts the rise T at frame 2685 (89.50833 s), place window 2685 to 2711, twin height 7.5e-4 (heights: 2690 8.4e-4, 2696 1.39e-3, 2698 1.84e-3, 2700 2.56e-3, 2709 1.4e-2). placeFrames 26 keeps the place weight above 0.5 to frame 2698 (the plates' rule). Dive T stays 2454 (81.80833 s), place window 2442 to 2454, twin height 1.7193e-3.
- Sky floor (the white strokes of mail 236). A carried terrain sky plate is a picture of the sky above the close view's horizon only; below it the plate is a mirror copy and then one row smeared down (Mars `sky-terrain.webp`, 3520 x 1980, about 27.6 rows a degree: true sky above row 483, mirror to 830, smear below 860). As the globe camera climbs, the limb drops and the smear shows above it: the strokes. `handover: { rise: { skyFloor: true } }` (or `dive`) fades the carried plate out below the twin's horizon: whole from `marginDeg` (0.25) under it, gone `featherDeg` (4) lower (`config.handover.skyFloor`). Off by default; applies only to a carried terrain plate whose own ring is none (a stop with its own ring keeps it). Mars has it on both sides. Rows: dive T twin dip 3.357 degrees, horizon rows 448 to 516, whole to 456 to 526, gone under 561 to 632; rise T dip 2.223, horizon 598 to 648, whole to 605 to 658, gone under 705 to 758.

## E2 as built (08:57), on Mars

Files: `src/core/place.ts` (layout, picture, pass), `src/diagnostics/probe.ts` (harness options on the diag string), `src/core/engine.ts`, `src/core/types.ts` (`PlaceState`, `Shot.place`, `Shot.skyShare`, `RenderView.place`, `RenderView.placeScale`, `StopSet.over`), `src/director/handover.ts` (`PlaceOptions`, `HandoverSide`, `skyWeight`, `skyTurnWeight`), `src/director/moves.ts` (`twin`, `twinFrame`, `closeUp`, `closePose`, `trapeze`, `glide`, `descent`, `ascent`, `coverBare`), `src/director/film.ts`, `src/core/caps.ts`, `src/terrain/relief.ts` and `src/diagnostics/diag.ts` (`uFine`, `uBias`), `config.handover.place` and `config.handover.descent`, `src/stops/mars/place.json`.

- The picture is the close side at T, whole: `director.shotAt(frameOfT, T, 'terrain')`, the world and near scenes of the stop's set, `post.vignette` 0 so no set undoes the lens in it. Camera = the close camera of T with a wider field (`placeCamera`: `setViewOffset`, same vfov). Two float targets, one cleared black (K) and one white (W); packed into one half float picture with mip levels: RGB = K, A = 1 minus the mean of W minus K (so exactly 1 where the set is opaque, 0 where only sky shows). `matte: 'alpha'` takes the set's own alpha from one render.
- Layout (`placeLayout`): `scale` whole texels per frame pixel (2; drops until the edge fits `min(place.maxEdge, device limit)`), margins per side from `valid: [left, right, bottom, top]` as shares of the frame (rounded up, at least 1 pixel) or `margin / 2` on every side; a half texel shift for even scales so texel centres sit on frame pixel centres. `place.maxShare` (0.5) bounds two opposite margins together; `caps.needs` asks for the buffer of that bound (2882 for the master format, inside the recorded 8192). Mars: 4496 x 2668.
- While the picture is drawn: `gradUniform` times scale and `setFine(scale)` (so `uFine` = scale and `uBias` = log2(scale) in the relief shader: mask and alpha lookups take the same mip level as in the frame, apron alpha gradients are per frame pixel), `view.place` true, `view.placeScale` the scale, `view.format` the frame format times scale. A set whose shader uses screen derivatives or implicit mip levels must scale them by `fineUniform` and `biasUniform` from `src/diagnostics/diag.ts` (both 1 and 0 outside the place picture).
- The pass (`Place.render`, after world, near and belt, before `over` and the cover): per pixel a ray against the unit sphere of the stop in camera relative coordinates; a hit projects the sphere point into the twin, a miss projects its direction as a point at infinity. Out = under * (1 - k) + k * (sky * (1 - A) + RGB * lens undo at this pixel). The sky is a copy of the sample target taken right after the sky and dust are drawn (`keepSky`); in the place window the world is drawn without the early pass.
- k = place weight * front * edge feather (inside the margin: `place.edge` of it) * `smoothstep(0, place.seen, honest)` * the enlargement limit (`place.maxEnlarge`, `place.enlargeSoft`). honest for a hit = (n . (twin - P)) / (n . (camera - P)) * height ratio: 0 at the twin's horizon, 1 at T everywhere; for a miss = (sin elevation seen from the twin + sin dip of the twin) / (the same for the camera).
- Sampling: `textureGrad` with the screen derivatives of the picture coordinate times `grad / scale`, capped at `place.maxGradTexels`. At T that is one texel at a texel centre: the close side's own pixel.
- The twin: `StopPlan.twin(local, time)`, a similarity from the close scene onto the unit sphere. Up of the close scene in terrain frame coordinates a = (0, cos tilt, sin tilt), tilt = horizonDeg + dipDeg, rolled by rollDeg; ground sphere radius rho = h0 / (1 / cos(dip) - 1), h0 = `terrain.ground ?? motion.terrainGround`. `twinFrame(time)` = {rotation: turn(time) * terrainFrame * R^-1 (R takes y to a), origin: the turned spot * (1 + h0 / rho), scale: 1 / rho}. A plan may override `twin` (Earth, Neptune do); the place, the pass, the globe camera at T and the sky carry all go through `plan.twin` only.
- The globe side of a stop that opted in (moves.ts): `descent(time)` and `ascent(time)` = `glide(far = hold pose or the pose after the rise, low = twin(close pose(time)), share)`, share = `trapeze` of the time between the far end and T (`descent.rise`, `descent.fall`). The glide keeps the look target (the point the twin looks at on the ground, at most `descent.targetReach` of the way to its horizon) and mixes log height, direction from the target and the rotation against the look frame. After T (dive) and before T (rise) the globe pose is the twin of the close pose, so the camera is C1 at T. E3 replaces this curve by a rate limited one.
- The sky through T (film.ts, globe side only): `Shot.skyShare` = `skyWeight` (0 until `skyFrames` before T, 1 at T); the glow is mixed to `terrainSkyGlow` by it, the skyLow factor fades out by it, both sky patches are passed with weights (1 - share) and share; the sky turn slerps `sky.orbits` to `sky.turns` by `skyTurnWeight` (0 at the far end of the segment, 1 at T, smootherstep); the sky quaternion is `slerp(1, E, turnWeight) * camera`, E = Qclose_world(tc) * Qtwin_world(tc)^-1 with tc the time clamped to the place window. At T the sky quaternion, the turn, the exposure and the glow are equal on both sides (tested).
- Near plane of the globe side of a stop that opted in: `min(0.0004, 0.2 * height)` as the floor, so the twin at 0.001 radii does not clip the ground.

### 00:47 to 01:20 (24 Nov, VM clock), THE LAW OF THE RISE (MAIL 432 point 3), mail 437 and 440

- What the harness measures as the speed of the picture (`tools/smooth/camera.ts`, and my own `paceOf` the same way): the mean of the unsigned px moves of 8 ground points under a ring of 96 px round the place, at least 4 of them. Model that fits the numbers: across the flow u = d ln(range), along the flow v = u - d ln(sin e), e the elevation of the eye over the ground of the place; the mean is about d ln(height) - 1.5 d ln(lift) where both have one sign (lift = sin e = centre.dot(out)). Near the ground with a low eye the tilt term rules, and where the flow turns over the speed has two humps (venus, earth, uranus, neptune).
- So on one share for height and direction the speed of the picture is steeper than the rate by the growth of the lift term. Mars's rise before: rate 0.073, turn 0.074, speed 0.097 at 2695 and 0.089 at 2726 (limit 0.08): red 4.
- THE LAW, built in `src/director/moves.ts` (`eyeLaw(kind)`, `glide(..., one: { swing, liftPower })`, `eyeOrbit`): three things per side. `pace`: the flanks of the bell of the height. `turn`: flanks of a bell of its own for the eye's direction from the place and the camera's uprightness. `liftPower` p: ln(height / lift^p) follows the pace bell instead of ln(height) (lift of the far pose and of the low pose taken out at the ends, so both ends stand where they stood). Each is the stop's own (`handover.KIND.pace`, `.turn`, `.liftPower` in its params), else the film's for that side (`handover.dive`, `handover.rise` of the config), else `handover.pace` for both bells and 0. Without a turn named anywhere `law.turn === law.pace` (one object) and with power 0 the old expression runs to the last bit. Both bells are refused where they are read if their steepest slope passes smooth.path.maxStep; the power must lie in 0 to 2. Checks of the config's side in `src/config.ts`.
- Flank orientation: x = 0 at the far end and 1 at the ground for dives and rises alike; `rise` is the far flank, `fall` the ground flank (in a rise the ground flank comes first in time).

### How a stop uses it

- Opt in: `handover: {}` in the plan's params (Mars: `src/stops/mars/plan.ts`, with `place.json`). Per kind: `at`, `dissolveFrames`, `placeFrames`, `skyFrames`, `live`, `picture`, `place: {scale, margin, valid, matte, lens}`.
- MAIL 221, a living close side: `handover: { rise: { live: true } }` (or `dive`). The picture is then drawn again for every sample with the set states (`time`, `local`, `u`) of the sample's own time and the camera fixed at the twin's pose of T; it is not cached. At T it is the close side of T.
- MAIL 222, tall things: (a) the set reads `view.place === true` in `update` and hides what must not ride in the picture (or keeps such things out of `world` and `near`); (b) the set gives `over: THREE.Object3D` in its `StopSet`; the engine keeps it at the set's position and rotation and draws it after the pass on both sides and in raw views, against the depth buffer as it stands, never into the picture. The stop draws the same thing with the same light on the close side, so E4's side difference counts it.
- No picture at all: `handover: { rise: { picture: false } }`. The sides still swap at T, the sky is still carried, no place state is made. For a stop whose set is the same true 3D object on both sides (the Sun asked). `placeFrames: 0` keeps the picture in the dissolve frames only.
- The field of the picture: `place.valid` = [left, right, bottom, top] margins as shares of the frame, the feather lies inside them (Mercury asked; `[0, 0, 0, 0]` gives one pixel). `place.lens` scales the lens undo of the pass (1 = what sets do).
- Own twin: override `twin(local, time)` in the plan class.
- Two corners a flank (02:58, 24 Nov): `handover: { rise: { pace: { rise, fall, corner, corners: { rise: [foot, top], fall: [foot, top] } }, turn: { ..., corners: {...} } } }`. `rise` is the far flank, `fall` the one at the surface view; foot is the share of the flank over which its slope comes in at the end of the move, top the share over which it goes out at the plateau; each above 0, the two at most 1 together. A flank not named keeps `corner` at both ends. A long foot at the far end lets the rate die away slowly. The film's law takes no corners.
- The picture's broad tone lent by the globe near its sides (17:50): `handover: { dive: { place: { toneIn: [left, right, bottom, top] } }, rise: { place: { toneIn: [left, right, bottom, top] } } }`. Each number is the reach of that side's feather as a share of the picture once the weight is 0 (0 to 4; at 0.5 and above the feathers of two sides meet, at 2 the whole tone is the globe's a few frames from T). Absent or all 0: off, the pass and its shader are the ones they were. No stop sets it (Mail 342: it stays off). It cures a picture that stands on the globe as a lighter or darker slab; it does nothing for a side that shows as a line of detail (that is edgeIn) or for ground that is not the same ground (that is the plates). Film wide numbers in `handover.place`: `tonePower` 0.5, `toneReach` 0.045 (the blur, of the frame's height), `toneCell` 8, `toneLandDeg` [1, 6]. Harness: `--diag "beauty/tone=2,2,2,2"` tries it on any frame of a stop without touching its plan, `tone=0,0,0,0` turns it off.
- Harness options on the diag string (`npm run still -- --diag "beauty/side=orbit/noplace"`): `side=orbit|terrain`, `shut` (every sample at the shutter middle), `t=SECONDS`, `noplace`, `mask` (the pass weight as grey), `picture` (the whole picture with its margins). File names carry the options.

Rise, frame 2700: mean 0.084, 99th 1, 99.9th 3, max 142; 0.72 percent over 1, 0.037 percent over 4, 122 pixels over 16. ## Decisions

- `StopPlan.risePose` (a property, the pose at the end of the rise) is renamed `riseEnd`, because the contract of E1 needs the method `risePose(time, view)`. One line outside my folders follows it: `src/stops/earth/plan.ts:91`. Told to /root with M1.
- T is snapped to a shutter middle. With `at` the share of the segment, `centreFrame = floor((start + at * length) * fps)`, the dissolve frames are `first = centreFrame - floor((n - 1) / 2)` to `last = first + n - 1`, and `T = ((first + last) / 2 + shutterFraction / 2) / fps`. For `n = 1` the draft sample of the dissolve frame lies exactly at T.
- The dissolve weight is constant inside a frame (taken from the frame number), so both sides share every part of that frame's shutter: `w = smooth((frame - first + 1) / (n + 1))` in a dive, `1 - that` in a rise. At the draft tier (one sample at s 0.5, threshold 0.472) the dive swaps to the close side at the first frame whose weight is above 0.472.
- The place window: dive `placeFirstFrame = firstFrame - placeFrames`, `placeLastFrame = lastFrame`; rise `placeFirstFrame = firstFrame`, `placeLastFrame = lastFrame + placeFrames`. `placeWeight` is continuous in time: dive 0 at `placeFirstFrame / fps` to 1 at `firstFrame / fps`, then 1; rise 1 until `(lastFrame + 1) / fps`, 0 at `(placeLastFrame + 1) / fps`.
- A stop that did not opt in: `handoverOf` null, `placeWeight` 0, `sideAt` is today's test (dive: terrain when `time >= centre`; rise: terrain when `time < centre`, centre = `coverWindow(seg).centerFrame / fps`).
- `forceSide` is a module switch read by `sideAt` only. The film path never calls it. The harness reaches it through a diagnostic option of the engine (E4). Motion measuring per side does not use it: `shotAt(frame, time, side?)` takes the side as an argument.
- Harness hooks live in my folders: `src/capture/frame.ts` and `src/main.ts` are not mine, so options ride on the `diag` string that `setDiagnostic` already passes to the engine.

## The contract of E1, for the stop builders

- Opt in: `MoveParams.handover` in the stop's `params.json` (or its plan). Its presence, even `{}`, opts in both the dive and the rise. `handover.dive` and `handover.rise` may override `at`, `dissolveFrames`, `placeFrames` of `config.handover`.
- `plan.divePose(time, view)` and `plan.risePose(time, view)` give either side at any time of the segment. `plan.dive(time)` and `plan.rise(time)` pick the side with `sideAt`.
- `handoverOf(segment)` gives `{kind, time, firstFrame, lastFrame, placeFirstFrame, placeLastFrame, frames}` or null. `coverWindow(segment)` is null for a stop that opted in; `judgedWindow(segment)` is what tools should use.
- T sits on a shutter middle: `centre = floor((start + at * length) * fps + 1e-9)`, `first = centre - floor((n - 1) / 2)`, `last = first + n - 1`, `T = ((first + last) / 2 + shutterFraction / 2) / fps`. Mars with the defaults: dive frame 2454 (T = 2454.25 / 30), place window 2442 to 2454; rise frame 2700 (T = 2700.25 / 30), place window 2700 to 2712.
- Inside a dissolve frame the weight is constant: `smooth((frame - first + 1) / (n + 1))` in a dive, 1 minus that in a rise. A sample at shutter position s draws the close side when `w > fract(s * 8 + floor(s * 8) * 0.618)`. With `dissolveFrames` 1 the weight is 0.5: half the samples of that one frame come from each side, and the draft tier (one sample, s 0.5, threshold 0.472) draws it from the close side exactly at T.
- `placeWeight(segment, time)`: dive 0 at `placeFirstFrame / fps`, smooth to 1 at `firstFrame / fps`, then 1; rise 1 until `(lastFrame + 1) / fps`, smooth to 0 at `(placeLastFrame + 1) / fps`.
- Softness (`motion.softFromPx`) and the cover do not exist in the dive and rise of a stop that opted in. Everything else in the film is as before, byte for byte.
- Once a stop opts in, `cut/CUES.md` and `cut/SHOTS.md` are stale until `npm run cues` and `npm run shots` run (both files belong to /root).

Part names stay (`<segment>-cover` in tools/pack.ts, tools/round.py, tools/keep.ts, tools/sheet.ts --cover). A helper beside `coverWindow`, for example `judgedWindow(segment)`: the cover window, or the handover window for a stop that opted in.
2. `tools/measure.ts`: G13 counts every frame of a dive and a rise of a stop that opted in. `--no-cover` stays as it is for the others.
3. `tools/cues.ts` and `tools/shots.ts`: for a stop that opted in they list T where they listed the cover's times, same format, so `npm run cues -- --check` still passes for the others and the sheet can be made again at the end.

5. Unit tests for 1 to 3 with one stop opted in and one not.

MAIL 221 (for E2): some close sides are alive (the Sun's cells boil, cloud decks drift, Jupiter's storm turns). A place picture drawn once at T freezes that life. So the place picture has a switch per stop and kind: `fixed` (drawn once at T, the default, cheapest) or `live` (drawn again for each sample with the set at the sample's own time, the camera fixed at the twin's pose of T). With `live` the globe side shows the living place from the first frame of the place window, and at T it is still equal to the close side. Determinism holds either way. The Sun's builder needs it for the rise at frames 180 to 269. Say in this notebook how a stop turns it on.

MAIL 222 (for E2): things that stand high above the ground cannot ride in the place picture (the Sun's loops, a moon, rings, a tall cloud tower). A stop must be able to (a) leave such things out of the place picture: a flag the set can read while the place picture is drawn, or a layer rule; and (b) draw them itself as true 3D on the globe side after the pass, in the same place, size and light as on the close side, so they are one object through T. At T the pass plus those objects must still equal the close side, so E4's side difference at T has to count them. Say in this notebook how a stop uses it.

Mail 332 (19:26, high): the floor (`handover.minShutter`) beside the cap, off by default, with two trial commits that differ in config only (14 px with 0.25 and with 0.5). /root sets cap and floor once for the whole film after its clips. The floor came before the review byte proof.

MAIL 351 (22:19 by /root's clock, 22:14 by mine), the work from here. In its words: "You own plans E and the engine side of F. Order: 1. PLAN E, first. One rate curve over the whole dive and over the whole rise, in place of a descent, a coast and a second move. Measured by the machines' handover reports on main (rate of fall or climb over the mean of the move): Jupiter's rise 0.73 at 3270, 1.53 at 3274 (a step of 0.46 in one frame), 1.75 to 3286, 0.50 at 3303; Jupiter's dive 0.79 at 2981, 0.69 at 3021, 1.07 at 3042; Mars's rise 0.34 at 2693; largest change in one frame Mars 0.18, Venus 0.18, Mercury 0.16, Earth 0.12, Uranus 0.10. Limits (they go into a new `smooth` block of the config, written by harness_builder; do not write the block yourself, give it your numbers): the rate has one rise and one fall; from one film frame to the next it changes by at most 0.08 of the move's mean; after its largest value it never climbs again by more than 0.03 of the mean and before it never falls by more than 0.03; the same step limit holds across both seams of the segment (hold into dive, dive into surface view, surface view into rise, rise into travel), so the move meets its neighbours with the same speed and direction. The turn of the camera and the speed of the picture (px a frame at the place) follow the same rule. Tests that hold all nine stops to it at whole and half frames. The stops' own plans must not be able to break it: say what a stop may still set. 2. The step at 3832 (Saturn's rise). saturn_coder measures: the plain engine has no step there, the capped shutter adds one of a frame, measured blur 18.5 to 21.9 px, shutter 0.759 to 0.640. The cap must be a continuous function of time: bound the ground plane in the motion measure or ease the measured blur over frames, and prove at half frames that the shutter has no step anywhere in the film. 3. PLAN F, engine. Both views drawn in one frame through the whole change, with depth between them; the border between globe and close ground set by distance on the ground, not by time; the sky, limb, haze and Sun one function of height on both sides (your limbSky folded in, the thin bright limb line on Mars cured); the place picture (`placeFrames`, `skyFrames`) and the dissolve retired stop by stop behind a switch per stop, and the handover report passing only with the place picture off. Nothing in the engine keyed to a frame number: every blend takes distance, height or angle. 4. Plan H touches you: every function of the engine must hold at fractional film frames (the capture runs at twice `film.fps`). travel_builder is the capture engineer and will change the frame interface, `engine.renderFrame` and Export; agree with it who edits `engine.ts` and `film.ts` when, and merge each other's commits often. ... Then work and push small commits to your branch; I prove them on the machines and put them onto main as they hold."
